| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExerciseDemonstrations.json` | Gym Visual IDs (no media) per catalog exercise, keyed by the frozen name: `gifMale`, `gifFemale`, `illustrationMale`, `illustrationFemale`, null where Gym Visual has no such version. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExerciseMedia/` | The gitignored drop folder for the licensed Gym Visual GIFs and illustrations (`<ID>.gif`, `<ID>.png`/`.jpg`): only its `README.md` and `.gitignore` are tracked, and the project excepts both from the app target, so the synchronized folder bundles whatever media is present and nothing else. Never commit media here (N-CRFL: no redistribution, no AI use). |

Since 2026-09-23 `ProgressPhotoSectionContent` decides the section's content in one place, and `ProgressPhotoLockNudgeCard` — an inline card above the capture control, never a modal — offers a user who skipped the onboarding lock step a lock that grants `.progressPhotos` only. |

Cites the reference ranges (Schoenfeld 2017, Baz-Valle 2022, WHO 2020, ACSM 2011). |

Information, not a streak: no grades, colours, trends or nudges. `WeeklyTotalsCopy` resolves its numbers and spoken copy through formatters and catalog keys. |

## Custom Clothing, Coins, And Friend Shop

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ZoomablePixelCanvas.swift` | `ZoomablePixelCanvas` — zoomable/pannable `UIScrollView` pixel-painting surface for the clothing editor (pinch-zoom, two-finger pan, one-finger paint), rendering via `ItemTextureRenderer`. Owns every touch that starts on it: its recognizers share only with the canvas's own zoom/pan, and every recognizer outside (page scroll, sheet drag, swipe-back) must wait for them to fail. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FriendShopView.swift` | `FriendShopView` — browse friends' post-session shops and buy items with coins (banned-seller filtered, with per-item report). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ClothingShareCodec.swift` | `ClothingShareCodec` — domain⇄wire codec for the in-person clothing shop: builds the device's own-designs `ClothingCatalogPayload` and re-sanitizes every incoming item before use. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ShopAlert.swift` | `ShopAlert` + `ShopAlertContext` — the three ways a shop listing can be refused (flagged name, listing cap, store ban) as alert cases, with the per-screen copy keyed by which screen is presenting. The shared home of the refusal alerts previously written out twice, in `CreationStudioView`'s confirmation step and `WardrobeView`'s swipe-to-sell path; in every case the item itself was already saved, just left unlisted. The store-ban case carries the ban's remaining time, and `ShopBanRemainingTime` renders it as one friendly, localized, rounded-UP unit ("30 days", "36 hours") in place of the old "after a while" (tracker §3.5). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/CoinLedgerService.swift` | `@MainActor @Observable CoinLedgerService` owning the coin ledger in memory with debounced append-only persistence. Its reset boundary is durable (2026-09-24): remembered device-locally before the rows are deleted, merged into every load and retried until it lands. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/CustomItemService.swift` | `@MainActor @Observable CustomItemService` owning the custom-item collection with debounced append/upsert persistence. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/MilestoneLedgerService.swift` | `@MainActor @Observable MilestoneLedgerService` owning the milestone ledger with debounced append-only persistence. Same durable reset boundary as the coin ledger (2026-09-24). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/CoinLedgerRepository.swift` | Per-row Core Data + iCloud coin-ledger store (`CoinLedgerRepository`), append-only JSON payload rows. Also holds the ledger's pending reset boundary in the controller's device-local `localDefaults` (`fernlet.coinLedger.pendingResetBoundaries`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/CustomItemRepository.swift` | Per-row Core Data + iCloud custom-item store (`CustomItemRepository`), append/upsert-only JSON payload rows. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/MilestoneLedgerRepository.swift` | Per-row Core Data + iCloud milestone-ledger store (`MilestoneLedgerRepository`); append-only in normal operation, plus the `deleteAll()` delete-everything wipe path (added 2026-08-20; since 2026-08-21 the service follows the delete with a `resetBoundary` marker append). Also holds the ledger's pending reset boundary in the controller's device-local `localDefaults` (`fernlet.milestoneLedger.pendingResetBoundaries`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/AppendOnlyRowStore.swift` | `AppendOnlyRowStore` — the shared upsert-only per-row Core Data engine (JSON `payloadData` rows keyed by a stable `idString`, sorted by `createdAt`, encoded via `RowPayloadCoders`) whose load/upsert bodies `CoinLedgerRepository`, `MilestoneLedgerRepository`, and `CustomItemRepository` previously carried as byte-for-byte clones. Upserts only the rows handed to it (a stale in-memory set cannot wipe rows synced in from another device) and deliberately exposes **no** delete path — deletion policy stays per repository. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/ClothingSharing/MeshClothingShop.swift` | Friend-mesh clothing shop (the composition's `clothingShop`): accumulates pairwise-sealed `.clothingCatalog` payloads from committed slots, then opens a 1-hour memory-only post-session browse window; receive/window/opt-out state only, never persisted. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/ClothingSharing/ClothingShopExchange.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.6**: the clothing shop's mesh wiring — its two inbound handlers (the catalog and the commit-symmetry request) on the mesh's payload token door, the commit-time offer and catalog request and the request answer through the mesh's captured-slot send, the once-per-slot send tracking and the request-answer rate limit, the outbound capability gate and the shop's two send test seams; the composition's session observer calls its commit, slot-loss and search-stop legs. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Wire/ClothingSharePayloads.swift` | In-person clothing-shop wire models: `ClothingCatalogPayload` carrying a peer's anonymous designer id, display name, and capped deterministically-ordered `CustomizationItem`s, and `ProximityClothingCatalog`. |

## Onboarding

Since 2026-09-23 also the post-age-gate intimacy choice (`ageCheckFinished()` → the step's second page; `complete()` commits it only when answered, different from the setting, and the 16+ gate is still open) and `DeferredLockSetupNudge`, the one-time progress-photo lock-setup nudge for a user who deferred the lock step — the first reader of `lockSetupDeferredKey`. |

| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/OnboardingStorageChoiceView.swift` | Onboarding step for choosing local vs. iCloud storage, with existing cloud data detection and migration summary; says when the account holds a Sealed backup or photo backup and how to restore it (bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §2). |

| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/OnboardingIntimacyChoiceScreen.swift` | `OnboardingIntimacyChoiceScreen` (2026-09-23) — the personal-details step's second page, shown only to a user the 16+ intimacy gate admits: a neutral "Keep intimacy tracking" (pre-selected, because it is what the setting already says) or "Turn it off", under the step's own "N of 8" caption and swapped in rather than presented as a sheet. Draft state until Continue; the answer lands through `FernletStore.setIntimacyTrackingVisible(_:)`, the Settings toggle's setter, and off hides — never deletes. |

## Lock And Privacy

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletLockUI/FernletLockGate.swift` | View modifier that gates protected UI behind the Fernlet lock state (hosts the no-passcode tap screen in its not-configured slot when given an opener). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletLockUI/FernletTapGate.swift` | The no-passcode Private tab's gate: `FernletPrivateHubOpening` seam, `FernletTapOpenOutcome`, `FernletUnopenableEntryCounts`, the one-button `FernletTapGateOverlay`, and the "Some entries can't be opened here" `FernletUnopenableEntriesCard`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/AgeAssuranceStore.swift` | `AgeAssuranceStore` — owns the device-local age determination behind the intimacy (16+) and mesh-chat (13+) gates. Persists to the never-synced sidecar; fail-closed on a fresh or undecodable record. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/AgeAssuranceRequest.swift` | The single seam to Apple's `DeclaredAgeRange` framework: one prompt for gates 13/16/18, plus the `.requestsAgeRange` view modifier. Every failure path lands on undetermined. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/AgeGateNotice.swift` | `AgeGateNotice` — the locked-state row for an age-gated feature: states the true reason, and offers a re-check plus the manual confirmation only when the system never ruled. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletLockUI/FernletLockView.swift` | Lock setup, unlock UI, numeric pad, passcode entry, and related lock interaction views. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletLock/FernletLockService.swift` | Lock service protocols, lock state, credential types, crypto provider, keychain item handling, audit logging, and lock/unlock orchestration. Also the no-passcode half of key custody (period-data design 2026-09-30, §4): the `.openedWithoutPasscode` state, the `deviceContentKey` row, `openWithoutPasscode(for:allowingMint:)` with its mint-safety proof (also run by a fresh passcode setup, and exposed read-only as `checkFreshKeyMintIsSafe(forPasscodeSetup:)`, whose `FreshKeyMintVerdict` tells entries no key can open from entries a recovery device still can), setup that ADOPTS the device key (salt written last, owner-checked, device row retired only after proof), `removeCredential(current:)` (refused while a superseded recovery enrollment is held), the shared launch/refresh state derivation and the interrupted-transition sweep. |

| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/KeychainHelpers.swift` | Low-level Keychain read/write/delete helpers (`KeychainItem`) shared by Fernlet's lock, journal and Worry Box keys, private-media keys, pending-narrative buffer, storage preferences, `FernletSocial`'s moderation bans and heart-drop keys and the app's sealed-backup escrow rows (`SealedBackupEscrowKey`). | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/FernletLockError.swift` | `FernletLockError` error enum for app-lock/passcode/biometric failures with localized descriptions. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletCrypto/ColumnCrypto.swift` | Shared ChaChaPoly column-encryption helper (`ColumnCrypto`) sealing/opening per-column values under a per-label derived key. Also the single home of the HKDF-SHA256 column-key derivation (`deriveColumnKey`, module-internal so production code reaches it only through the sealing methods) since the duplicate `FernletLockCrypto.deriveColumnKey` was deleted — the label and the derivation are part of the at-rest format, so changing either orphans every sealed column. ProximityKit's two mesh stores seal through ProximityKit's byte-for-byte copy, `ProximityColumnCrypto`, since its plan step A0.2.9; `ProximityNamespaceGoldenTests` holds the two to each other. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletCrypto/CryptographicPurpose.swift` | `CryptographicPurpose` (an opaque, unforgeable domain value whose initializer is `fileprivate`) and `FernletCryptoPurpose`, the 81-entry registry of the app's signature, KDF, HMAC, AEAD and hash domains (since ProximityKit plan step A0.2, 38 of them are twins of the labels ProximityKit reads from its namespace, and two, the heart and presence pair salts, of the feature salts `.fernlet` declares; since plan step A1.0, 6 of the signature labels Fernlet mints with ProximityKit's `featureSignature(_:framing:)`; all pinned equal by `ProximityNamespaceGoldenTests` until they retire). Purpose spellings are FROZEN protocol data: each is an input to a shipped key, digest, or signature, so renaming one orphans what it protects. Also owns `TranscriptFraming` and `signingBytes(_:)`, the positional check that a signed transcript really begins with its reviewed domain. Full inventory and change procedure: [Crypto-Domain-Separation.md](Crypto-Domain-Separation.md). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletCrypto/DeviceBindingID.swift` | Per-install random ID (ThisDeviceOnly keychain row) passed by `ColumnCrypto` as AEAD associated data (column purpose ‖ this ID) on every sealed-column write, format v3; fail-closed since the crypto standardization round's Phase 3 (no durable ID, no seal), and the open tells an absent row from a retryable read error (`currentForOpen()`). | `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletLock/SecureEnclaveContentKeyWrap.swift` | ECIES wrap of the lock content key under a non-exportable Secure Enclave P-256 key. Additive while `LockKeychainKey.wrappedContentKey` exists (LEGACY / SE-less hardware); AUTHORITATIVE and the only recoverable copy once the service has proven a round-trip and deleted the scrypt item (HARD-BOUND — Docs/Verifiability.md §4/§6.1, done). `unwrap` fails soft to nil for the maintenance paths; the hard-bound recovery path uses `unwrapResult` instead, so a destroyed enclave key (`FernletLockError.contentKeyUnrecoverable`) is never confused with a keychain that would not answer (`.contentKeyTemporarilyUnavailable`). |

It owns the Sealed backup v2 `engine` (built over the period adapter); the period entry points are façades over it: `setSealedBackupEnabled` (on: owe the upload and run an export now if the Private tab is open; off: `beginDisabling` stops the engine FIRST, then deletes — or, from the user's switch with the slot observed as another iPhone's, keeps it — so nothing is written after the delete), `restorePeriodBackup` (restore phase only), `settlePeriodBackup`, `restorePeriodBackupHere` / `replacePeriodBackupWithThisIPhone` / `startNewPeriodBackup` (in-memory intents carried out now or at the next hub settle), `releaseRestoreHoldForOwner` (released on the tap, then an owner-release pass), the escrow adopt (marks the v2 payloads owed and nothing more) and the Retry (an ambient engine pass). (Until B2/B3 the journal and intimacy payloads kept the v1 paths — `reconcileJournalBackup` / `reconcileIntimacyBackup`, the empty-store-only restores, `preResetCopiesBlockedByNewerEntries` / `replacePreResetCopyWithThisIPhone` — all gone now.) **Unit B2:** the intimate-log backup is on the engine too (`IntimacyBackupAdapter`, over the app's ONE intimacy funnel handed over by `attachIntimacyLogStore`, which installs the host's visibility gate and the dirty-marking mutation hook): the intimacy enable, turn-off, Retry, escrow adopt and owner release route to the engine through the generic v2 façades (`restoreV2Backup`, `settleV2Backup`, `restoreBackupHere`, `replaceBackupWithThisIPhone`, `startNewBackup`, `removeUnopenableEntries`; the period names stay as thin wrappers); the intimacy arms of `mayReuploadFromLocalStore` and `isEmptyStoreForRestore`, `reconcileIntimacyBackup`, the targeted intimacy restore and the per-call intimacy store are gone; `applyRestoredChunks` merges intimacy chunks (v1 or v2) through the adapter; the pre-reset "blocked" check is journal-only. **Unit B3:** the journal backup is on the engine too (`JournalBackupAdapter` over `FernletStore`'s sealed journal repository — injected through the new `journalRepository:` init parameter, resolved lazily — and the journal device key read without minting from `journalDeviceKeyService`); every live payload is now v2, so the v1 machinery is gone: `reconcileJournalBackup`, `mayReuploadFromLocalStore`, `isEmptyStoreForRestore` / `RestoreScope` / `isFreshInstallForRestore`, the targeted journal restore, the launch pass's journal arm, `preResetCopiesBlockedByNewerEntries` / `replacePreResetCopyWithThisIPhone`, `journalNarrativeCount`, `makeIdentity` and the `storeNotEmpty` / `emptyLocalStore` wiring errors; `restoreJournalBackup` is the journal's engine restore; the launch pass only sweeps the retired payload and reconciles the escrow key; `applyRestoredChunks` merges every live payload through its adapter (the journal's follow-up rebuilding day skeletons). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupEscrowKey.swift` | `SealedBackupEscrowKey`, the X25519 escrow key every Sealed backup and own-photo backup is sealed under, kept in iCloud Keychain beside the device identity's rows at content-addressed accounts (`backupEscrowPrivateKey.k.` + SHA-256 of its public key; the legacy fixed account read, never written), and ProximityKit's `IdentityProvisioningParticipant` for the identity it rides: provisioning's Case 1 adopts the escrow present before the key-agreement row is rewritten device-only, Case 2 adopts it once the mint lands, Case 3 promotes a pre-migration build's synced key-agreement key into its synchronized slot BEFORE the mint overwrites the row (`identity.escrow.legacyPromoteFailed` and no mint when that write fails), Case 4 adopts nothing, a wipe drops it; the WS-1 to WS-4 lifecycle moved verbatim from `IdentityService` (the sealing mint, device-only and verified, the open path's load that never mints, the candidates restore tries, the launch reconcile with its additive legacy migration and add-then-delete promotion, the user-confirmed conflict adoption, the v1 and v2 HKDF derivations under `FernletCryptoPurpose.KeyDerivation.sealedBackupLegacyV1` / `.sealedBackupV2`), over FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem` and `FernletAuditLog` under the `identity.escrow.*` events; the one shipping file that writes a synchronizable row. Its `IdentityService` extension is the app's one identity factory, `fernletApp(keychainService:)` (Fernlet's namespace and a fresh escrow key; `FernletStore.makeProximityIdentity()` answers it), the escrow API the app calls through the identity (`localBackupEscrowPublicKey`, both `sealedBackupKey`s and `sealedBackupKeyCandidates`, `provisionBackupEscrowKeyForSealing`, `loadBackupEscrowKeyForOpen`, `reconcileBackupEscrowKey`, `adoptSyncedBackupEscrowKey`, each answering "no escrow" without the participant), `BackupEscrowReconcileOutcome` and the `nonisolated` `escrowKeychainAccount(forPublicKey:)`. |

Also `SealedBackupTrigger`, `SealedBackupV2Status`, `SealedBackupV2Phases`, `SealedBackupV2GateFailure`, `SealedBackupV2PassReport`, the background-task seam (`SealedBackupBackgroundTaskAsserting`, `SealedBackupUIKitBackgroundTasks`) and `StoragePreferences.isSealedBackupEnabled(for:)`, the one payload-to-switch helper. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupV2Engine+Restore.swift` | The restore phase (§4.2 R1–R10): the owner hold stops every trigger, `.forOpening` only, head fetch → G → no head resolves the restore even with no escrow key (a head with no key waits for the synced one) → open inside the adapter's seam → a changed "Restore it here" set returns to held → rollback check (bypassed only for exactly the chosen stamp, and for this install's accepted set; a refused set is remembered for Privacy & Data's two choices) → suffix by set → G → open and verify the set (writer, set, salt, total; v1/v2 never mixed) → G → merge with the live key → bookkeeping only while the epoch is unchanged. `classifyRestoreFailure` (a key mismatch is a retryable wait; a set that authenticates but whose envelope or records this build cannot read — `decodingAuthenticated` turns a `DecodingError` there into `SealedBackupV2FormatError.unreadableRecords` — is `.needsNewerFernlet`: status set, no export follows, not retried in this process; B3 fix round 1; a failure once G fails — a call a quiesce cancelled — is a stop that records nothing), `openHead`, `SealedBackupV2Stop`. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupV2Engine+Export.swift` | The export phase (§4.2 X1–X6): E1 (waived only by a confirmed Replace or Start new), the owner hold, "Remove them" (re-classified, only shown ids still dead), spacing, E2 writer-first (`headVerdict` / `classifyHead` / `unopenableVerdict` — the §5.5 table: unchanged metadata decrypts nothing; this install's own set (v2 writer tag, v1 signing key) up to the highest generation it recorded (rollback floor, accepted head, in-flight), its accepted SET (generation and salt prefix) or the exact replaced stamp pass; an own set numbered above that reopens the restore and merges it first (`reopenRestoreForNewerOwnSet`, an iPhone put back from an older device backup of itself); "Start a new backup" passes only over a set that does not open; anything else is held and observed; a failure once the work was stopped records no `.failed`), and the escrow mint rule (`mayMintEscrowKey`: never while any enabled v2 payload has a head, unless Start new). |

| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/DuressRecoveryCoordinator.swift` | `DuressRecoveryCoordinator` (+ `DuressRecoveryRequest` / `DuressRecoveryReply` / `DuressRecoveryTranscript` / `DuressRecoveryDecision` / `DuressRecoveryOutcome` / `DuressRecoveryError`) — the app-side half of `DuressMode.recoveryLock` (security-hardening Phase 7, step 8): the in-person QR mutual-auth ceremonies that enroll the user's own second device as a recovery custodian and later get the content key back from it. Lives in the app target because it needs BOTH `FernletLock` and `ProximityKit` and the lock must gain no ProximityKit edge — the content key is released into `IdentityService.seal` through a closure, never returned. One type plays both roles (custodian displays, primary scans). Transport-free: every step returns the bytes to send. **No cloud route of any kind** — recovery is in-person mesh + QR only. Since ProximityKit plan step A0.2.5 it scans and builds, signs and checks the verify response under its identity's namespace (`identity.namespace`, `identity.purposes`), which makes `fernlet.verify.response.v1` ProximityKit's label, supplied by Fernlet's namespace; the two duress labels stay the app's: since plan step A1.0 `DuressRecoveryTranscript` frames each transcript under its FernletCrypto entry and signs and checks it under its twin, `requestPurpose` or `replyPurpose`, minted with ProximityKit's `featureSignature(_:framing:)` (raw prefix). |

The page it is pushed from is `.appLockSettings`-gated, and since 2026-10-16 (DPA-76) the entry sheet also opens on the real passcode, checked through a counted `.appLockSettings` unlock and again by `configureDuress(pin:mode:current:)` — the "must be different from your passcode" refusal had been a free passcode oracle on a page left open. `DuressSetupAvailability` is the view-free gating policy — `.recoveryLock` unselectable without an enrolled custodian, the recovery device unremovable while `.recoveryLock` is armed — split out so those two rules are unit-testable. `DuressModeCopy` is the destructive-path copy, reviewed as a unit; service rejections are surfaced verbatim, never restated. |

Transport is a **QR relay**, not the mesh: the coordinator is transport-free, so the bytes ride codes on screen and ProximityKit gains no new `PayloadType`. The relay adds no trust — the small hops carry only public nonces and public keys (a substituted key produces a response the real scanner rejects), and the large hops are already sealed and signed by the coordinator. |

| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/PrivacyDataSettingsView.swift` | Privacy and data settings screen for managing storage preferences, HealthKit toggles, and sealed backup options. Routes every destructive/OFF toggle through a pre-commit warning; surfaces the sealed-backup restore status + escrow-conflict banner (retry / re-link). Also carries the own-photo escrow switch (with its size disclosure) and the step-5c "Lock photos to this device" consent ceremony — the branch that lets a user device-bind their own photos without turning the iCloud photo backup on. Holds the "Forget corrected searches" row (research §26 fix 1.10's escape hatch — clears the local correction memory and nothing else, behind the same `DestructiveConfirmation`, hidden when nothing is remembered). After an app-lock reset it says the Sealed backup is kept as it was and offers the owner's "Restore" (it releases the hold), in place of upload promises the hold makes untrue. Entered through the same fresh device-owner check with or without a Fernlet passcode (period-data design Q5; a missing iPhone passcode admits only a no-passcode install), with the delete card beneath the check. Names the period backup's states (§10.6): another iPhone's set — or a v1 set, which names no iPhone — ("Restore it here" of exactly that set behind its confirmation, "Replace it with this iPhone's history" behind a destructive one), a restore waiting for its key from iCloud Keychain and a set sealed with a backup key this iPhone doesn't have (each with "Start a new backup" behind its own confirmation, which says to wait for the key unless iCloud Keychain was reset or the backup is damaged; the "other iPhone keeps its own entries" Replace confirmation only for a named set), entries that cannot open, and "will catch up the next time you open the Cycle page". Releases, on entry, a reset hold that keeps no enabled backup's copy (no false "Restore" prompt). **Unit B2:** the intimate-log backup speaks through `SealedBackupV2StatusRows` with `.intimacy` words (`FernletStore.intimacyBackupRowState`; nothing while hidden, under 16 or in duress): "Restore it here" / "Restore anyway" behind a non-destructive confirmation, Replace, Start a new backup and Remove them behind destructive ones, "Open Private to finish." announced once; its turn-off confirmation keeps a slot observed as another iPhone's ("Stop backing up this iPhone's intimate logs?") and otherwise says the backup is deleted for every iPhone that uses it; the Retry predicate no longer ORs in the intimacy flag (R2-F9), and the intimacy pre-reset copy is never "blocked" (a merge). **Unit B3:** the journal backup speaks through the same rows with `.journal` words (`FernletStore.journalBackupRowState`; nothing during a duress session), its restore confirmation naming the fork rule ("If an entry was changed on both iPhones, you'll see both versions") and that deleted entries may come back; the journal turn-off confirmations mirror the intimate-log ones; the Retry predicate no longer ORs in the journal flag, the old "hasn't finished uploading" line and the pre-reset "can't be restored" lines are gone (every restore is a merge); one generic confirm/run path (`confirmV2BackupChoice`, `v2DestructiveConfirmation`, `runV2BackupChoice`, `v2RestoreAlert`) serves both kinds. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupV2RowState.swift` | `SealedBackupV2RowState` — the ONE mapping from a v2 payload's engine status (and, after a relaunch, the persisted owner hold, restore marker, observed head and owed upload, in that order) to a Privacy & Data row (design 2026-09-30, §10.1): none, finishing ("Open Private to finish."), waiting for the restore, waiting for the key, another iPhone's set, older than seen, sealed with another key, damaged, needs a newer Fernlet (also for a restore that found a set only a newer Fernlet reads — never the "next time" promise, B3 fix round 1), paused, too large, failed, catch-up. Used by the intimate-log rows (unit B2); the journal joins in B3. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupV2StatusRows.swift` | `SealedBackupV2StatusRows`, `SealedBackupV2RowCopy` and `SealedBackupV2RowChoice` — the intimate-log (unit B2) and journal (unit B3) Sealed backup rows inside Privacy & Data's banner (design 2026-09-30, §10.1): one whole-sentence catalog entry per `SealedBackupV2RowState`, written per kind in its `SealedBackupV2RowCopy` (`.intimacy`, `.journal`; never a spliced noun) with its frozen identifiers (`privacy.sealedBackup.<kind>.<state>`, the catch-up line keeping `…intimacyDeferred` / `…journalDeferred`), one accessibility element per row, 44 pt buttons with hints ("Restore it here", "Replace it with this iPhone's …", "Restore anyway", "Start a new backup", "Remove them"), the choice handed to the parent to confirm. Shown only while the kind is visible and no duress session runs. (Was `IntimacyBackupStatusRows.swift`.) |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/DestructiveConfirmation.swift` | Reusable destructive-action confirmation (`DestructiveConfirmation` + `.destructiveConfirmation` modifier): the mutation runs only on an explicit destructive-role confirm, so no destructive settings toggle can ship without a warning. |

## Data, Persistence, And State

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/` | Core domain models (formerly the app's `Models.swift`), split per area — see the Domain Models section. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore.swift` | Main observable app store that coordinates repository data and app-level state changes. Holds the ordered `deleteAllData` wipe funnel, whose **leg 0** (P6 item 4's fix review) calls `MeshNetworkManager.beginPrivacyWipe()` — which switches the routed projection off for the rest of the wipe, advances the session-content generation and tells the session observer, whose chat drops the transcript — paired with `endPrivacyWipe()` in a `defer`. Builds and owns `meshFeatures`, `FernletSocial`'s `FernletMeshFeatures` composition (the mesh is `meshFeatures.mesh`, also `meshNetworkManager`), and wires the features' providers and hooks on the objects that own them. **P7 item 2:** owns `applyProximityRunPolicy(scenePhase:protectedDataAvailable:duressSessionActive:now:)` (the `appLockEngaged:` label retired by P9-3-A, 2026-09-22) — THE run-policy funnel and the only writer of `MeshNetworkManager.applyRoutedAccessGate(_:now:)` outside ProximityKit (the lock facts are parameters, read at the edge, never the lagging mirrors) — plus the `selectedTab` mirror, the memory-only `deleteAllInProgress` bracket flag raised inside the wipe funnel, and the kept `proximityRunVerdict`. **P7 item 3** split the funnel into a private core (`runProximityPolicy(_:now:)` — input, verdict, the gate write, then the seams' executor; since session photos U3 fix round 1 (U3-L-U3-R1) the input is sampled TWICE, the gate written from the first and the radio half — and the kept verdict — decided over a second sample taken after the gate's pass, because that pass can make an ended session's photo review outstanding and so raise `sessionPhotoReviewBlocksDiscovery`) behind three entries: the scene edge (as before, retaining its three facts), the view edge (`applyProximityRunPolicy(duressSessionActive:now:)`, a fresh duress fact over the retained scene facts) and the store edge (`reapplyProximityRunPolicy(now:)`, everything retained; before any scene edge both assume the most restrictive scene). The two nearby opt-in setters and the wipe funnel's raise and lower of `deleteAllInProgress` run the policy instead of calling a manager; `discoveryTimeoutTask` (the tab's five-minute timeout) lives here for the seams. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/AIRetryQueueService.swift` | AI analysis retry queue; stores pending retry records and calls `onChange` on each mutation. Extracted sub-service of `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/DerivedSignalsService.swift` | Derived signal computation and deferred post-launch rebuild scheduling. Extracted sub-service of `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/DerivedSignalsRebuilder.swift` | Pure helper that rebuilds derived signals from a historical day window; used by `DerivedSignalsService`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/SavedRecipeService.swift` | Observable saved recipe list manager; loads, adds, deletes, and persists saved recipes. Extracted sub-service of `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/SnapshotSaveCoordinator.swift` | Debounced snapshot persistence coordinator and remote-change reload handler. Extracted sub-service of `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/StoreCore/PendingWriteBuffer.swift` | `DebouncedRowBuffer` / `DebouncedAppendBuffer` — the queue-then-debounce-then-flush write machinery, and its durability contract (the pending queue is the sole un-persisted copy, so it is cleared only after a confirmed write and a failed write keeps it for retry instead of trapping), shared by `SavedRecipeService` / `CustomItemService` (upsert+delete rows) and `CoinLedgerService` / `MilestoneLedgerService` (append-only rows). The one home of what those four services previously carried as byte-identical copies; row meaning, load-time dedup, and reset semantics stay in each service. Plus `PendingResetBoundaries` (2026-09-24): the durable reset-boundary half both ledgers share — remember before the delete, land and retire, merge what is still pending on every load. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStoreLoader.swift` | Async store bootstrap coordinator that manages `preparing → ready → failed` phase transitions at launch; its once-per-process `startIfNeeded()` first runs the app's own `ProximityLiveActivityReaper.endOrphans()` (`ProximityLiveActivityReaper.swift`, before any proximity manager can exist), then loads the store. Imports no ProximityKit since plan step A0.6.4, when the reaper became the app's. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStoreAccess.swift` | `FernletStoreAccess` — the one process-global store-acquisition cache, shared by the scene loader (via `ExchangeIntentService.loadStoreForUI`) and the background App Intents. Main-actor isolated and single-in-flight: a cold background launch and the scene loader coalesce onto one `load()` rather than building competing `FernletStore`s over the same repositories. Lifted out of `ExchangeIntentService.swift` by the P10 mesh round so the companion background-refresh handler can share it. Its missing `deinit` is allowlisted by `MemoryLifecycleBoundaryTests` (ML1) with the invariant that makes it safe. **P10 item 4** adds the LATE ATTACH (decision D-10.4.1): the cache returns its store whatever arguments a later `load()` passes, and the scene loader passes the app's one long-lived `HealthKitService` while every background caller passes nil — so a cold background wake that built the store first left the foreground on a store with no gateway for the rest of the process, its workout sync silently falling back to a second instance of its own. `load()` now attaches a caller's gateway to a cached or coalesced store that has none, through `FernletStore.attachHealthKitServiceIfMissing(_:)`, and audits when it does. The repair lives here rather than in the refresh handler because the handler may not speak a HealthKit spelling at all (§16.4's wall), and because the background App Intents have had the same defect since they were written — the refresh only moved it from a rare Shortcut to a fifteen-minute cadence. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/DataExportBuilder.swift` | `FernletDataExport` + `FernletStore` builder — assembles the user's non-sealed data into one human-readable JSON export file (allowlist projection; sealed/sensitive data excluded by construction); each meal carries its food data's licence attribution (`attribution`, 2026-10-18). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/TrainerExportBuilder.swift` | `TrainerExportBundle` / `TrainerExportOptions` / `TrainerExportWindow` + `FernletStore` builder — curated fail-closed-allowlist workout + nutrition export for a trainer/nutritionist, with opt-in extras. Also carries macro targets, training setup (location/equipment/split, and since 2026-10-20 the session length and each location's weights on hand), the body areas trained around (`profile.trainAroundAreas`, one line of frozen area tokens; added 2026-10-20 as optional keys, so older readers and the logged lines `ExerciseLineParser` reads are unchanged), and the per-exercise progression rollup (`ExerciseLineParser`). The window defaults to unlimited (file/mesh) and narrows for the clipboard. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletStore+DemoSeed.swift` | DEBUG-only `FernletStore` extension that seeds today's diary with representative demo content (day-idempotent) so every tab renders populated cards for the UX appearance UI tests. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/LocalFernletRepository.swift` | Local JSON-style repository contract and implementation, snapshot/database records, derived signal creation, limits, and memory engine logic. `loadAllDaysWithUnreadable()` does not account for every row in read-only recovery (B3 fix rounds 1 and 2). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/CoreDataFernletRepository.swift` | Core Data-backed repository implementation. `loadAllDaysWithUnreadable()` / `readAllDays()`: the day history that says what it could not read (an undecodable row's day named in `unreadableDayKeys`; not accounting for every row under read-only recovery, a failed day-row fetch or a row with no date key; a memo of a read that did not account for every row is never served — B3 fix rounds 1 and 2). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/Persistence.swift` | Core Data persistence controller setup for runtime and previews/tests. Owns `localDefaults`, the store-scoped device-local key/value surface (`UserDefaults.standard` for the default store, in-memory otherwise). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/StoreLocalDefaults.swift` | `StoreLocalDefaults` (the three `UserDefaults` accessors, which `UserDefaults` itself conforms to), `InMemoryStoreLocalDefaults` (a lock-guarded per-controller stand-in for in-memory and explicit-URL stores) and the shared `PendingResetBoundaryCoding` — the device-local, never-synced surface the ledgers keep their pending reset boundaries in, scoped exactly like the store so a test store never writes production defaults (tracker §3.6, 2026-09-24). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/StoreLocalDefaults+UserDefaults.swift` | The one-line `nonisolated extension UserDefaults: StoreLocalDefaults`, alone in its file on purpose: the persisted-surface wipe wall scans receiver-less accessor calls in any file declaring `extension UserDefaults`, and would read the protocol's own requirement declarations as unlabelled defaults writes. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/CoreDataModelBuilding.swift` | `CoreDataModelBuilding.makeAttribute` — the shared `NSAttributeDescription` factory for the two programmatic (no `.xcdatamodeld`) model builders, `PersistenceController` (CloudKitSync) and `PrivatePersistenceController` (PrivateStoreCore), each of which previously held a private byte-identical copy. Pure in-memory construction that names no sealed or synced entity, which is what lets it sit at layer 0 and be reused from both sides of the S3 wall. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/CloudKitDataService.swift` | CloudKit record read/write service and `ExistingDataSummary` detection used during onboarding migration, with `SealedBackupPresence` — which Sealed backups and photo backups the account holds, by their heads' and manifests' existence (`sealedBackupPresence()`, bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §2); the sealed-backup record layout — the head under `sealed-backup.<payload>`, v1 suffix chunks `…chunk.<i>`, v2 suffix chunks scoped to their set `…chunk.<i>.<set>` (`saveSealedBackup(_:setTag:)`, `sealedBackupSuffixChunks`, `sealedBackupSuffixIsPresent` (the commit's verify, by record ID with no field or asset), `pruneSealedBackupSets` (never the set the current head names), `parseSealedBackupSuffixName`; the delete matcher knows both forms); `CloudKitRecordDatabase.existingRecordIDs` (a no-keys existence fetch). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/StoragePreferences.swift` | Codable model for user storage preferences: iCloud sync, local backup exclusion, HealthKit capability toggles, and sealed backup flags. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/PeriodTrackerStore.swift` | The cycle S3 funnel after the cutover (period-data design 2026-09-30, §6.3): the frozen cycle vocabulary, `PeriodLogOutcome` / `PeriodDeleteOutcome`, the Apple Health mirror seam (`PeriodHealthKitServicing`) and lock seam, and `PeriodTrackerStore` — save seals first and mirrors second, edit in place, delete Fernlet's rows before Health's copies, load with fill-on-read and dedupe, drain (v1 and v2 payloads), the Health-only day actions, the writer epoch. The held legacy-import task has no `deinit` cancel: `MemoryLifecycleBoundaryTests` (ML1) allowlists it with the invariant that makes that safe. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleDayEntry.swift` | `CycleDayEntry` (a day's records + Fernlet's Health copies with no record + other apps' samples, with derived accessors), `CycleHealthSamples` (the one rule turning Health samples into a clinical block / record), `FernletCycleRecordMirror` (the frozen `FernletCycleRecordID` metadata key) and `CycleLegacyIdentity` (deterministic legacy record ids). §6.4, §7.1, §8. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleLegacyImport.swift` | The legacy cycle import (§8): `CycleLegacyImportLedger` (the two persisted halves, set done by delete-all and reset) and `PeriodTrackerStore.runLegacyImportIfNeeded` — narratives and unmarked own Health samples into records in one merge write, rechecked after every await, the unopenable notes named for the Cycle page's card. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleMirrorDeletion.swift` | `CycleMirrorSampleKind` (the five Apple Health sample kinds a cycle mirror holds; in-memory, unfrozen; `kinds(writtenFor:)`, `possibleCopyKinds(of:)`) and `CycleMirrorDeletion` (a mirror delete's count and the kinds Apple Health refused — reported, not thrown, so a kind the entry never held neither stops a rewrite nor reads as "still in Apple Health"). Review round 1, R2. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/MenstrualNarrativeRepository.swift` | Persistence for menstrual narrative entries (the legacy source the cycle-record import retires). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleRecord.swift` | `CycleRecord` — one cycle entry sealed as ONE blob (day, clinical block, narrative block; each block nil = unknown), its frozen tolerant Codable (`"v": 2`), `CycleRecordOrigin`, the block types and the one merge rule `CycleRecord.merged` (whole blocks by clock; commutative, idempotent, associative; the origin follows the copy that speaks most for the clinical block, `combinedOrigin`). Period-data design 2026-09-30 §5.1. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleRecordRepository.swift` | Sealed at-rest CRUD for `CycleRecord` under `fernlet.cycle-record.v1`: the one `upsertMerged` write path (insert / merge / replace-dead / refuse-undecided, retiring legacy narratives in the same save), the edit, classified pages (dead on an id mismatch), keyless count/ids/deletes, bounded at 20 000 records. §5.2, §6.1. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/CycleRecordStore.swift` | `@MainActor CycleRecordStore`: the fail-closed, visibility-gated funnel for every cycle-record read/write, the mutation hook and counter, and the backup seam (`backupChunk` / `restoreMerging` / `withBackupSeam`, plus `isStoreHealthy`; `backupPrePass` is not on the v2 engine's path). §6.2. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateStoreCore/PendingNarrativeBuffer.swift` | Codable buffer for pending menstrual narrative payloads (v1) and whole cycle records (v2, `cycleRecordJSON`); holds at most 200 and refuses the next with `.full` instead of evicting. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/SavedRecipe.swift` | Saved recipe model and repository implementations, including legacy JSON migration support. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/HealthKitGateway/ActivityTypeCatalog.swift` | Workout activity type search and HealthKit activity type mapping. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/FernletRepository.swift` | The abstract `FernletRepository` persistence contract for snapshot/day/tier-two load and save, and `loadAllDaysWithUnreadable()` → `DayHistoryRead` (`days`, the `unreadableDayKeys` of rows that would not decode, and `accountsForEveryRow` — false in read-only recovery, after a failed fetch or with a row that has no date key; the default is for stateless doubles only — B3 fix rounds 1 and 2). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/FernletSnapshot.swift` | `FernletSnapshot` persisted aggregate value type serialized to/from storage. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/HealthKitStorageStrip.swift` | The HealthKit half of the storage strip (HealthKit information is never stored in iCloud, 2026-09-23): `FernletDay.strippingHealthKitValues()` / `healthKitResidue` / `overlayingHealthKitResidue(_:)`, the device-local `DeviceHealthResidue` value, and the `DeviceHealthResidueStoring` cache contract (which since the body-profile follow-up also carries the imported body profile). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/DeviceHealthBodyProfile.swift` | `DeviceHealthBodyProfile` — the age, sex, height and weight THIS device imported from HealthKit, kept in the device cache instead of the synced `settings.userProfile` (2026-09-23): `applied(to:)` (the overlay, re-clamped), `removingFieldsEdited(from:to:)` and `typedProfile(_:editedFrom:to:)` (a profile-screen edit writes only the changed fields to settings). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/DeviceDaySummaryStoring.swift` | `DeviceDaySummaryStoring` — the contract for the device-local store of the AI day summaries (2026-10-15: `FernletSnapshot.storedDailyScores` strips `daySummaryText` from every persisted copy, so the summaries stay on the device that wrote them): read all (nil while unreadable), record/remove one, clear, and the one-time legacy-capture marker. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/RemoteChangePublishingRepository.swift` | `RemoteChangePublishingRepository` refinement exposing an iCloud remote-change `AnyPublisher`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/DayRecordRepositoring.swift` | Per-day persistence protocol (`DayRecordRepositoring`) plus the sanitize-barrier `DayRecordUpsert` mint. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/SavedRecipeRepositoring.swift` | `@MainActor` append/upsert-only saved-recipe persistence protocol (`SavedRecipeRepositoring`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/CoinLedgerRepositoring.swift` | `@MainActor` append-only coin-ledger persistence protocol (`CoinLedgerRepositoring`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/CustomItemRepositoring.swift` | `@MainActor` append/upsert-only custom-item persistence protocol (`CustomItemRepositoring`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletPersistence/MilestoneLedgerRepositoring.swift` | `@MainActor` milestone-ledger persistence protocol (`MilestoneLedgerRepositoring`); append-only, deliberately no delete on the protocol — the wipe's `deleteAll()` lives only on the CloudKitSync conformer. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/DayContentSummary.swift` | `DayContentSummary` precomputed content-count roll-up carried in the aggregate blob for single-read existing-data detection. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/DerivedSignalFactory.swift` | `DerivedSignalFactory` computing the rolling derived-signal window (mood/energy/eating/progression/micronutrient trends) from stored day pairs. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/LogRecords.swift` | Derived-table DTO records (`DailyLogRecord`) persisted alongside the local database. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/TierTwoMemoryEngine.swift` | `TierTwoMemoryEngine` deriving capped tier-two behavioral memories from the rolling 14-day window. Its texts reach the companion prompt as "user pattern:" lines, so they describe and never judge; every branch's text passes `DiagnosticLanguage` (2026-10-16: two said "period" and were never stored). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/TierTwoMemoryStore.swift` | `TierTwoMemoryStore` — the device-local, never-synced, always-backup-excluded JSON sidecar the tier-two memories live in (owner decision 2026-09-23); refreshed by both repositories after a successful save, purged by delete everything; `deviceWindow(restoringHealthKitTo:from:limit:)` puts this device's HealthKit residue back into the refresh's window (2026-10-18). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/LocalPersistence/FeelingTagMoodScale.swift` | `FeelingTag.moodScore` — the fixed 0.2–1.0 mood scale shared by the deterministic derived engines, and the single home of the two previously duplicated private copies in `DerivedSignalFactory` and `TierTwoMemoryEngine`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/DayRecordRepository.swift` | Per-row Core Data + iCloud day-history store (`DayRecordRepository`) with duplicate-row dedup by `dateKey` — the rows MERGED by entry id (`DuplicateDayRows`, the read-side stop-gap of bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §3) and the merge written into the newest row before an older one is deleted; `loadAllWithUnreadable()` names the day of every row that would not decode and says whether every row was accounted for (the fetch succeeded, every row has its date key) — B3 fix rounds 1 and 2. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletFoundation/RowPayloadCoders.swift` | `RowPayloadCoders`: single source of truth for the persisted-JSON encoder/decoder config (sorted keys + ISO-8601 dates, with opt-in pretty-printing for the human-readable on-disk files). Moved down to layer 0 from `CloudKitSync` so the local-only blob file in `LocalPersistence` shares it too; it replaced the per-store private `makeEncoder`/`makeDecoder` pairs that had drifted (the coin-ledger and custom-item row stores once wrote bare numeric dates). ISO-8601 truncates to whole seconds — callers comparing dates across representations must account for that. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/MultiDeviceSyncWarning.swift` | Pure `MultiDeviceSyncWarning` classifier for the "devices will diverge without iCloud" warning state. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/SealedBackupRecord.swift` | Sealed-backup transport DTOs (`SealedBackupRecord`, `SealedBackupPayloadType`): the opaque encrypted CloudKit record shape. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/SealedPhotoRecord.swift` | Own-photo escrow transport DTOs (`SealedPhotoCorpus`, `SealedPhotoSlot`, `SealedPhotoManifest`, `SealedPhotoRecord`): one encrypted record per photo id plus the sealed per-corpus manifest (id set + per-id content hash + the progress corpus's index sidecar). Born at record format 2 — no legacy shape to tolerate. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/DiaryStore/DiaryStore.swift` | `@MainActor @Observable DiaryStore`: the portable diary-state slice (scoring, meal/recipe/workout/journal/settings) carved from `FernletStore`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateStoreCore/PrivatePersistenceController.swift` | `PrivatePersistenceController`: dedicated local-only (never-iCloud) Core Data store for sealed, encrypted entities. Its model is versioned since 2026-09-30: V1 (the four shipped entities, frozen and checksum-pinned) and V2 (+ `CycleRecord`), with a V1 → V2 staged migration over in-memory model references on the store description. The bump is one-way: an earlier build opening a V2 store migrates it down and drops every `CycleRecord` row. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateStoreCore/PrivateRowPlumbing.swift` | `PrivateRowPlumbing.deleteRows` — the shared keyless fetch → guard → delete → save → history-prune sequence that the sealed repositories' `deleteAll()` methods (`JournalNarrativeRepository`, `WorryNarrativeRepository`, `IntimacyLogRepository`, `MenstrualNarrativeRepository`) each repeated inline. Rows are deleted without decrypting them, so deletion stays available while the app is locked or the feature is hidden, and the (rethrowing) persistent-history prune is part of the sequence. Deliberately not used by `purgeEncryptedEntities()`, whose wipe must stay atomic across every sealed entity under one save. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/IntimacyLogRepository.swift` | Sealed Core Data repository for `IntimacyLog` records (ChaChaPoly column-encrypted intimacy notes). Since unit B2 (Sealed backup v2, §8) it also carries the backup surface: the keyless id snapshot `allIDs()` (total order, bounded), the classified `logs(ids:contentKey:)` → `IntimacyLogPage`, the one restore write `upsertMerged(_:contentKey:)` → `IntimacyLogMergeResult` (insert absent ids with their own stamps and a capped note, keep openable rows and only fill a missing Health link, replace dead rows, never delete; one save, rolled back on any throw; idempotent), the keyless `delete(ids:)`, `isStoreHealthy` and `IntimacyLogRepositoryError`; `insertAtomically` is gone. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateHealthStore/IntimacyLogStore.swift` | `@MainActor IntimacyLogStore`: the fail-closed, visibility-gated funnel for every intimacy sealed-note read/write. Since unit B2: the mutation hook (`attachMutationHook`, run after an insert, a Health link, a merge that changed anything, a delete that removed rows) and the Sealed backup v2 seams — gated `backupChunk(ids:contentKey:)`, `restoreMerging(_:contentKey:)`, `withBackupSeam(_:)` (throw while hidden or keyless, never answer empty); ungated `allIDs()`, `isStoreHealthy`, `delete(ids:)`. `backupPage` and `restore` are gone. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMemoryStore/JournalNarrativeRepository.swift` | Sealed journal-narrative store: `JournalNarrative` model, `JournalNarrativeStoring` protocol, and its encrypted repository. Its Sealed backup v2 surface (design 2026-09-30, §7, unit B3): `isStoreHealthy`, the keyless `allIDs()`, `ids(onDays:)` (every entry on the days whose day row will not decode, which the snapshot keeps — B3 fix round 2) and `skeletons(ids:)`, the classified `backupRecords(ids:hubKey:deviceKey:)` (`JournalBackupPage`; hub key, then the device key `JournalBackupDeviceKey`; an unknown tag needs a newer build, never dead) and THE restore write `upsertMerged(_:hubKey:deviceKey:)` (`JournalNarrativeMergeResult`: insert with the backup's stamps unless an entry on its day is already that entry — the same words and creation stamp, `JournalMergeDayEntry.isCopy(of:)`, B3 fix round 1 — keep an entry that opens, fork a different text unless an equal one is on its day, replace dead, never delete, idempotent; replaced `insertAtomically`); `apply` keeps a restored entry's stamps. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMemoryStore/WorryNarrativeRepository.swift` | Sealed, device-only Worry Box store: `WorryNarrative` model, `WorryStoring` protocol, and its encrypted repository. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PeriodContextBridge/PeriodContextBridge.swift` | Period-module egress bridge: raw→abstract cycle conversions (`PeriodPhaseBand`) exporting only coarse enums past the S3 wall. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PeriodContextBridge/PeriodPhaseTrendEngine.swift` | `PeriodHealthTrend` device-sealed per-phase wellbeing trend (coarse direction + confidence band) from the user's own history. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/HeartDropCloudTransport.swift` | CloudKit **public**-database ferry for heart drops (the app's only public-DB use): upload one sealed drop per tag, fetch a friend's tag window with per-chunk anti-starvation budgeting. Sees tags and ciphertext only. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/SealedBackupGenerationStore.swift` | Device-local, never-synced high-water mark of the highest sealed-backup generation this device has written or accepted, per payload type **and per own-photo corpus** (a separate key namespace, since the two routes have independent write histories) — the state half of the rollback defense. The crypto half is the generation binding in `SealedBackupCrypto.authenticatedData`. `mintNext` (persist-before-upload) is the v1 exports'; a v2 set's generation is recorded through `recordAccepted` only on a verified commit or an accepted restore. Cleared by the delete-all path (the v2 accepted heads are not in it and are kept). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/CloudKitSync/CloudKitSchemaDeploy.swift` | The schema-deploy launch flag. `isRequested(arguments:)` is pure and testable; the caller that actually pushes the schema is `#if DEBUG`-gated so it cannot fire in Release. See [CloudKit-Schema-Deploy.md](CloudKit-Schema-Deploy.md). |

## Domain Models (FernletDomainModel)

The portable value-type layer that replaced the app's old `Models.swift`, split per area.

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WellbeingModels.swift` | Day, health-context, journal, sleep, hygiene, goals, and daily-score value models (`FernletDay`, …). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/NutritionModels.swift` | Nutrition, food, meal, recipe, and macro/micronutrient value models (`UserNutritionProfile`, …). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FoodPortionReader.swift` | The tolerant USDA portion reader (ingredient-search round F4a): a portion's leading measure word decides ("cup, sliced" is a cup, "medium (7\" long)" a size, "clove" a count noun; frozen English tokens) and `eachPortion(in:)` picks what "1 each" means — the medium among several sizes, the single named count, or the one its NLEA/RACC serving names. A count unit on a USDA yield ("piece … (yield from 1 lb raw meat)") and a named count holding a part or packaging word ("apricot half", "small box") are not read (fix round 1). Also `FoodPortion.recipeUnit` (exact reading first). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/RecipeHouseholdMeasure.swift` | How a household recipe amount is saved (F4a): "1 each" of a banana becomes `118 g` plus the additive optional display metadata `householdMeasure` ("medium", 118 g per one) — grams every build and peer resolves; re-opened as "1 each" in the editor and shown as "1 medium (118 g)". A line an older build already reads the same ("1 cup" of a food stating one cup) is kept as typed; applied by the editor, a substitution fork and a meal-log recipe (`RecipeDefinition.savingHouseholdAsGrams`) (fix round 1). F4b adds `labelHead`/`labelQualifier`/`headTakesPlural` (frozen `pluralNouns`), so a caption's noun agrees with its count ("2 eggs", "2 cups, sliced", never "2 mediums"). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/RecipePortionPicker.swift` | The recipe editor's per-food unit menu (ingredient-search round F4b): `RecipePortionOption` (a unit, one of the food's USDA portions, a USDA typical size or the person's own size) and `RecipePortionPicker.choices(for:personal:)` — the food's own portions first as what one is with its grams ("1 medium (118 g)", "1 cup, sliced (150 g)"; `FoodPortionReader.householdLabel(of:)`), then personal sizes, then typical sizes where the food states none of that kind, then only the units that convert; a unit whose one IS a listed portion is folded into it (`standIns`). Also the amount carried across a pick and how a saved line re-opens (`reopened`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/TypicalPortionTable.swift` | Curated USDA typical sizes (F4b, report §6.3 Rungs C/D): 76 frozen English keys — produce, eggs, chicken and butter counts, baking and pantry cups and spoons — every weight cited to the SR Legacy row and portion text it is read from (verified against the shipped catalog). A row IS an entry's ingredient by its F5 head noun, its key words and none of its excluded (processed-form) words; offered only where the food's own data has none of that kind, badged as an estimate; spoons derive from a stated cup's density. Read by the recipe editor and the web importer's fallback, never by the converter. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutModels.swift` | Workout, exercise, muscle, and equipment value models (`Workout`, …). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutProgram.swift` | Workout program/training model value types: `ExperienceLevel`, `WorkoutProfile` (with the injury picker's `injuryAreas`), `WorkoutLocation` (parks a newer build's equipment tokens and keys; its weights sync at the settings top level), `WorkoutSafetyFilter`, training-split selection, and the day-plan engine. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutInjury.swift` | The structured injury input: `BodyArea` (frozen tokens, each area's muscles and movement patterns, the spine's barbell rule), `InjurySeverity`, and `InjuryLimits` — the picker, the note and the older avoided sets combined (picker wins for its areas; severe leaves the area's work out, mild leaves out only its heavy end and eases the rest). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/InjuryNoteMatcher.swift` | `InjuryNoteMatcher` — reads the free-text injury note by whole words (plurals included), skipping idioms such as "getting back into lifting", "back to back", "hip hop" and "six feet tall". |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutRecoveryGuard.swift` | `WorkoutRecoveryGuard` — the 48-hour same-muscle guard: moves to a split day that spares muscles worked hard yesterday (unless the overlap is the split's own order), else takes a set off the overlapping lifts, with a plain note. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ExerciseHowTo.swift` | `ExerciseHowTo` + `ExerciseHowToCatalog` — the bundled, localized how-to text for all 118 catalog exercises (3–5 cues, one common mistake, one easier version) keyed by the frozen name, and `displayName(for:)`, the one lookup a screen draws an exercise name through (`ExerciseTarget.displayName`, `PrescribedExercise.displayName` / `displayLine`). The text lives in `ExerciseHowTo+Push/Pull/Legs/Core/Conditioning/Stretches.swift`, every string a `exercise.<keyStem>.*` catalog key. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutTimedSessions.swift` | `WorkoutTimedSessions` — reads a plan's cardio and mobility lines (`TimedDescriptor`: steady, intervals, mobility flow, the rest day's gentle stretch; anything uncertain stays text) and expands them when a run starts: `CardioSegment` countdowns (`CardioPace`, rounds, follow-on parts with no lead-in), the owner's warm-up order as a 30–45 s stretch session, an eight-minute floor stretch; plus the activity type a timed line logs as. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutPlanRun.swift` | `WorkoutPlanRun` — a planned workout's line as a guided-runner step, as written: timed lines left for `WorkoutTimedSessions`, sets × reps and holds under the catalog's name, anything else a step marked done; a weight the line names preselected as written, else the load engine's suggestion for a catalog lift; the line kept on screen when it says more than the step. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/HeartRateZones.swift` | `HeartRateZones` — five heart-rate zones from age (Tanaka, 208 − 0.7 × age) and, when trusted, Apple Health's resting heart rate (Karvonen heart-rate reserve; percent of maximum otherwise), and each cardio pace's target zone (easy and steady → 2, hard → 4). Pure. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutSessionBlocks.swift` | `WorkoutSessionBlocks` — the blocks around a lifting session: the owner's warm-up stretch flow, the activation block (light core, balance, low-rep jumps), the coverage-chosen cool-down, and the plan text that summarizes them. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutShortWarmUp.swift` | The short warm-up: about four minutes of the owner's flow, a subset in its own order — the opening fold, then the stretches for the areas the day's lifting works (`StretchArea`), then the flow's order. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutSessionOptions.swift` | The session preferences (synced, frozen tokens, parked decoding): `WorkoutSessionLength` (no limit, 20, 30, 45, 60 min), `WorkoutWarmUpMode` (full, short, off), `WorkoutCoolDownMode`, and `WorkoutSessionOptions`, which the planner reads. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutSessionBudget.swift` | `WorkoutSessionTime` — a session's time by the runner's own rule (sets, holds plus lead-ins, rests, descriptor minutes, a 60 s changeover before each exercise after the first), shown on the plan card. `WorkoutSessionBudget` — fits a session into its length in the owner's order (short warm-up, cool-down to two, accessory work, accessory sets, main-lift sets to two; main lifts never dropped), or notes how long it runs; caps a cardio descriptor's minutes. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutExercisePreferences.swift` | `WorkoutExercisePreferences` — exercises the planner never suggests and saved alternates it prefers (frozen names, synced at the settings top level, tolerant bounded decoding); `WorkoutExerciseSwapOptions` — the plan editor's ranked stand-ins. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/CustomExerciseDraft.swift` | `CustomExerciseDraft` (the form's state → an `ExerciseTarget` with every catalog field), `CustomExerciseIssue` and `CustomExerciseLibrary` (name checks, the shared 500 cap, add / edit / delete). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ExerciseTraitNames.swift` | Localized display names for the catalog's curation tokens: `MovementPattern`, `MovementPlane`, `ExerciseModality`, `ExerciseDifficulty`, `ExerciseMeasure`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutRestGuidance.swift` | Evidence-based default rest-between-sets table (`WorkoutRestGuidance`) keyed by movement `Demand` and training goal. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutLoadModels.swift` | Load-aware progression's value types: the frozen tokens (`LoadUnit`, `ExerciseEffort`, `OverrideReason`, `EstimateConfidence`, `CalibrationStep`), the persisted `ExerciseResult` (on `Workout.exerciseResults`; per-set weights when they differed, unknown keys from newer builds parked and written back), the engine's `LiftExposure` (judged at its heaviest set's weight when the sets differed), and `WeightPrescription` / `LoadGuidance`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutSetLog.swift` | Set-by-set records after the fact (idea 6): `LoggedSet` (reps and weight; a blank repeats the set above), `ExerciseResult.record(_:unit:)` (a correction), `ExerciseResult.handLogged(...)` (a lift entered set by set, recorded as a guided run records it), and `Workout.replaceExerciseLine(_:with:)`, which keeps the readable line in step with the record. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutLoadEngine.swift` | `WorkoutLoadEngine` — Epley-with-reserve estimate, confidence and the range it sets, and the next prescription from the last result (more reps, add weight now, repeat, lighter, after a break, recalibrated, pain and equipment holds, the override question). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutPlateau.swift` | `WorkoutPlateau` — the stall rule (adherence, no new best over four sessions from the range, not fatigued or explained by overrides), the staged response (`PlateauStage`), the rest check (`RecoverySummary`), and the helper-muscle list. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutCalibration.swift` | `WorkoutCalibration` — the monthly strength calibration: when it is due, the submaximal test, the one-set strength check, and the opt-in true max; on a location's own loads when its weights are set. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutWeightInventory.swift` | `WorkoutWeightInventory` — a location's "Weights I have" (dumbbell pairs, kettlebells, a bar and plate pairs; sorted, deduplicated, bounded) and the barbell totals they make; `WorkoutWeightPresets` (dumbbell presets, standard bells, bars and plates per unit); `LoadLadder` — the loads a lift can use there, with nearest-load lookup and the runner's step. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutLocation+Weights.swift` | The synced shape of the weights: `workoutLocationWeights` at the settings blob's top level (location id → entry), decoded tolerantly — junk skipped, a newer build's keys parked, an unreadable entry kept verbatim, an orphan dropped. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutLoadEngine+Inventory.swift` | The engine on real equipment: snapping to a location's loads, the light-load guard (`lightLoadGuardRatio`, 10 %), and `LoadCap` — reps first, then a set, then the jump, when the next load is too big or missing. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutProgram+Load.swift` | The day plan's load pass and `WorkoutLoadContext`: per-lift prescriptions, variation and support-work swaps, the easier session and longer rest, and calibration expansion. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutWarmUpSets.swift` | `WorkoutWarmUpSets` — warm-up ramp sets before a heavy main lift (idea 12): which lifts qualify, the ramp (about 40 % × 8, 60 % × 5, 80 % × 3 of the working weight, on loadable weights, never under the bar or at the working weight), the single-set `.ramp` steps the load pass puts in front, the level default for the setting, and the seams (`isWarmUpSet`, `removingWarmUpSets(from:)`, `reattach(_:from:)`, `isRampLine(_:)`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/BarbellPlates.swift` | The plate calculator (idea 12): `BarbellPlateSet` (a bar and plate sizes; `standard(_:)` is 45 lb / 20 kg with the standard plates), `PlateCalculator` (greedy per side, exact or the closest makeable load, ties to the lighter) and `PlateLoadout`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutProgram+LoadNotes.swift` | Which session note the load pass writes: what changed on a lift, the plateau story, the calibration brief, and the count-based ladder line that replaced "Week N" (the sentences are `WorkoutPlannerCopy` keys). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutPlannerCopy.swift` | `WorkoutPlannerCopy` — every planner session-note sentence as a String Catalog key (2026-10-20, idea 16): the plural-ruled count phrases, the session notes, the 48-hour guard's and the time budget's notes, and the rest-day note. Composed in the device's language at plan time; stored notes are never re-translated. `WorkoutPlannerCopy+Load.swift` holds the load pass's sentences (progression, weights on hand, the rep ladder) and `WorkoutPlannerCopy+Plateau.swift` the plateau story, rest check and calibration brief. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutPlannerNames.swift` | Draw-time names for the planner's FROZEN English names: `WorkoutSessionNames` (session titles and "Morning · Easy cardio"), `SessionTime.displayLabel`, `WorkoutSplitDayNames` / `WorkoutSplitDay.displayTitle`, `TrainingSplit.displayName`, and the day plan's and session's drawn names. `WorkoutPlannerNames+Slots.swift` names the slot labels (`WorkoutSlotNames`) and `WorkoutPlannerNames+Locations.swift` the built-in locations and presets (`WorkoutLocationNames`, `WorkoutLocation.displayedName` / `savedName(editing:)`, `LocationTemplate.displayName`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/WorkoutPlanLine.swift` | `WorkoutPlanLine` — draws the planner's frozen plan-text descriptors ("Easy cardio - 20 min", the warm-up and cool-down summaries, the fallback circuit) in the user's language, minutes kept; the stored plan text never changes. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/CompanionModels.swift` | Companion appearance, workshop, and texture value models (`WorkshopData`, `TextureEntry`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/CompanionEmotion.swift` | `CompanionEmotion` — the companion's momentary feeling (11 FROZEN tokens + display forks + "Okay, feeling sleepy"); deliberately not `Codable`; `isWidgetPublishable` keeps five in the app. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/SettingsModel.swift` | `FernletSettings` app-settings serialization aggregate (hydration, companion, equipped items, designer provenance). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/AgeAssurance.swift` | `AgeGate` (13/16/18), `AgeGateVerdict`, `AgeAssuranceProvenance`, and `AgeAssuranceRecord` — the pure age-gate rules: fail-closed, a below-gate verdict is unappealable, and provenance is asymmetric (it can close a gate but not open one). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/NavigationEnums.swift` | Screen/widget/shortcut navigation enums (`FernletScreen`, `ConnectionInspectorMode`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ScoringValueTypes.swift` | Pure scoring value types (`ScoringWeights`) carved out of the app-layer scoring logic. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FoodItemSearch.swift` | Pure relevance-search value logic over `FoodItem` plus a restaurant-chain brand lexicon (`FoodItemSearch`, `FoodBrandLexicon`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FoodIngredientIdentity.swift` | The recipe surfaces' ranking (ingredient-search round F5): `FoodSearchRanking` (`.standard` everywhere by default, `.ingredientIdentity` for the recipe editor's typeahead and the swap sheet) and `FoodIngredientIdentity`, which decides from a row's own name whether it IS the typed ingredient — the head noun of the first comma segment, plural-aware; a USDA class's member ("Cheese, parmesan", "Squash, zucchini"), read from the third segment when the second only qualifies the class ("Squash, summer, zucchini", "Chicken, broilers or fryers, thigh"); a phrase ending at a preposition; coordination; part nouns ("Ginger root"); one-word parenthetical synonyms ("(cilantro)") — so "Cookies, chocolate chip" and "Bread, banana" never count. A product name is one phrase (a preposition makes it a composite; a first segment followed by anything but an echo of its words or a variant word — "Salsa, Mild", "Chocolate Chips, Semi-sweet" — is a flavor list, so "Lemon, Ginger Drink" is no lemon); a person's own food is read by its first segment ("Chicken breast, grilled"). Two levels: a USDA reference row that writes a typed modifier as its kind ("Milk, whole" for "whole milk") leads the other USDA rows — read after history and source, so a person's own and logged foods still lead. Off while the head is still being typed (a trailing single letter, or under one ranked row in ten saying it whole). Frozen English word lists. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FoodSearchHistory.swift` | Research §26 fix 1.9's history-first ranking input (`FoodSearchHistory`): the foods this user has logged, weighted `log(1 + count)` × exponential recency decay (τ = 21 days), read as the TOP key of `FoodItemSearch`'s comparator on the typed-query surface only. DERIVED from `DiaryStore.recentMeals` on every write and at init — no new persisted surface, so the diary's own wipe is the feature's wipe. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/RecipeSourceURLMatcher.swift` | The one source-URL normalization rule (scheme/host case-insensitive, fragment stripped, query kept) behind the zero-network duplicate skip, the supersede-on-re-import match, the superseded-photo cleanup, and the mesh already-saved decision. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/EnumDecodeCompat.swift` | Forward-compatible decode helpers (`EnumDecodeCompat`) for synced raw-value enums: park and re-adopt unknown tokens via side channels. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/AIAnalysisRetryRecord.swift` | Codable retry-queue record (`AIAnalysisRetryRecord`) for pending AI-analysis payloads; element type of `FernletSnapshot.retryQueue`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/AIDestination.swift` | `AIDestination` enum naming where an AI call is routed (on-device FoundationModels or web nutrition lookup). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/TierTwoMemoryRecord.swift` | Codable tier-two behavioral memory record (`TierTwoMemoryRecord`) referenced by the repository and memory agent. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/DiagnosticLanguage.swift` | Pure best-effort classifier (`DiagnosticLanguage`) flagging clinical/diagnostic language to screen out of AI prompts and stored memories. Covers all sixteen of spec §8's terms (2026-10-16); "manic" matches as a whole word (`wholeWordPatterns`) so "manicure" does not. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/JournalMemoryCapture.swift` | Core Memory's journal capture (owner decision 2026-09-23): `MemoryNote.emotionOnly(for:)` mints a journal entry's memory as its `FeelingTag` token with NO text, and `JournalMemorySummaryPolicy` is the test an AI summary must pass to become that memory's text (bounded, no diagnostic language, not verbatim / a prefix / an excerpt). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/CustomItemModels.swift` | User-designed custom clothing/accessory models: `ItemSlot` taxonomy, palette-indexed grid textures, `CustomizationItem`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ItemDesigner.swift` | `ItemDesigner` provenance value carrying only an anonymous, stable designer id for "designed by …" attribution. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ItemNameModeration.swift` | Pure profanity/slur name gate (`ItemNameModeration`) for items listed for sale in the clothing shop. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ClothingModeration.swift` | Value types and pure verdict math for the in-person clothing-shop report/ban moderation ledger (`ReportReason`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ModerationBanRecovery.swift` | `BanEvidence` + `ModerationBanRecovery` — the pure half of lifting a store ban when its reporters withdraw (2026-09-24): which recorded evidence a `retract` positively withdrew (absence never counts), the bounded canonical merge, and the monotone uncapped threshold test. Reporter identities arrive as opaque tags through a closure, so the module stays crypto-free. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/ClothingShopLimits.swift` | Shared constants and a wire-boundary sanitizer (`ClothingShopLimits`) clamping untrusted peer-received shop items. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/CoinEconomy.swift` | Append-only coin-ledger model (`CoinLedgerEntry`, `CoinLedgerKind` earn/spend) and balance-aggregation math with structural idempotency. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/MilestoneLedger.swift` | Append-only cumulative care-milestone ledger model (`MilestoneEventKind`, `MilestoneLedgerEntry`); lifetime counts, monotonic between wipes (since 2026-08-20 "delete everything" clears the ledger, reversing the earlier survive-reset rule; since 2026-08-21 the wipe also appends a `resetBoundary` marker and aggregation counts only rows whose day is at/after the marker's day AND created strictly after its instant, so neither re-synced pre-wipe rows nor rows re-derived from re-synced days can resurrect the trail). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/IdentityDedup.swift` | `Array.deduplicatedByID()` — the application-level union-merge that collapses duplicate-id rows (first seen wins, order preserved), needed because `NSPersistentCloudKitContainer` mirrors by record identity and does not honor an id attribute as unique. The one implementation now behind `CoinEconomy.deduplicatedByID`, `MilestoneEconomy.deduplicatedByID`, and the StoreCore per-row services' load paths, which each held their own copy of the loop. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/Closeness.swift` | Deterministic closeness math and close-friend slot assignment: `FriendInteractionDayCounts` and capped daily warmth points. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/FriendState.swift` | Friend-facing fuzzy wellbeing state (`FriendFuzzyState`) and its sealed shareable payload, derived from the state enum, never the score. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletDomainModel/HeartSharing.swift` | Wire model (`HeartPayload`) and presentation math for proximity "send good vibes" hearts between friends. |
