The composition's session observer runs its legs at each moment; `purgeHeldSessionPhotosForDeleteAll()` and `invalidateEncryptionKeyCaches()` are its delete-all legs. `removeReportedPhoto(_:)` (2026-10-15, App Review guideline 1.2): a reported photo is removed the way declining it would — a held one answered as discarded, a wall one deleted with its tombstone. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Mesh/MeshSessionReviewTypes.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.11**: the session-end review's three values — `MeshFriendReviewBatch` (the ended session's keep-as-friend candidates, ProximityKit's `MeshSessionRosterEntry`, and the photos awaiting the person's choice), `SessionPhotoAnswerFailure` and `SessionPhotoAnswer` (what one answer did, per photo; `keptOnWall` only), which the app and `FernletProximityUI`'s review sheet read. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Mesh/MeshRoutedPhotoBody.swift` | P5 item 13: `MeshRoutedPhotoHeader`/`MeshRoutedPhotoBody`, the sealed plaintext of a routed photo in the family's frozen framing — the header and the resized JPEG RAW — with the family's two header allowances (`maxHeaderJSONByteCount`, `maxFramedHeaderByteCount`) as the body's own constants, which the photo row's cap formula reads. No `keyEpoch`, no sender-supplied identity; a `MeshRoutedBody` the engine's one opener decodes. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Mesh/MeshContentLedger.swift` | P4 item 7 (plan §10.3): Fernlet's content kinds over ProximityKit's generic union — `MeshMergedPhoto` and `MeshMergedMessage` (the heart kind is `HeartSharing/MeshMergedHeart.swift`) — and `MeshContentLedger`, the value that holds all three; in-memory projections, nothing persisted and nothing wire. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Mesh/MeshContentIngest.swift` | P4 item 7, second half: what happens to merged content at the receiving member — `MeshPhotoReassembly` (hash validation at reassembly), `MeshContentGates` (the age gate and the local block/ban re-run at ingestion as a view filter over an unmutated union) and the heart receipt (its commit through the ledger is `HeartSharing/MeshHeartCommit.swift`). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Messaging/MeshSessionChat.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.9**: `MeshSessionChat`, the live-session chat (the composition's `chat`) — the transcript (`sessionMessages`), the 13+ gate (`chatAllowedProvider`), the text sender `sendTempMessage(_:)`, which answers `MeshTextSendOutcome` and publishes nothing, the per-origin message budget, and the temporary-message row's projection handler (a private adapter: the 13+ gate before the open, then the engine's session liveness, the budget and the transcript). Since 2026-10-15 (App Review guideline 1.2) the sender runs `SessionMessageFilter` over the sanitized draft before the frame and the mint and answers `MeshTextSendOutcome.filtered` (test seam `sendFilterList`); the projection threads the author's verified key into the transcript; `refreshVisibleTranscript()` re-folds the view gates at once after an in-session report. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Messaging/MeshRoutedTextBody.swift` | P6 item 4: `MeshRoutedTextHeader`/`MeshRoutedTextBody`, the sealed plaintext of a routed text in the family's frozen framing, the text RAW to the end; the text family's 1 KiB header allowance as an extension of ProximityKit's `MeshRoutedItemBodyFormat`; FIVE hostile shapes on the one `malformed` token, the fifth a `senderName` above the 128-byte bound refused on the wire. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/FernletSocialCopy.swift` | `FernletSocialCopy` — the module's copy vault (the `FernletUICopy` model): the one string `FernletSocial` hands out resolved, the camera's hold-failure line, which the session photos publish on the mesh's `meshError`, through `bundle: .module` against the module's own `Localizable.xcstrings`; its key keeps the `proximity.camera.` spelling. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/RecipeSharing/ProximityRecipeShareManager.swift` | Fernlet's recipe share (`@MainActor` `@Observable`, `FernletSocial`'s since ProximityKit plan step A0.7.3) over ProximityKit's `ProximityPairSession`, built at construction under `FernletConnections`' recipe profile on the host's own namespace (`init(store:)`; the internal `init(pairSession:)` takes a built session and makes itself the session's payload handler and its event sink, capturing itself weakly). The share sheet's observables are the session's, read through (`nearbyRecipients`, `sendState` rendering the typed status, `lastShareOutcome`, `engagedRecipientID`), and so is `isListening`, which is not observed: the run-policy seam reads it on each run; its own state is the diagnostics ring (`ProximityRecipeShareDiagnosticEvent`, `ProximityRecipeShareDiagnostics`, declared here and shared with presence) and the inbound queue. `sendRecipeShare(_:to:)` hands the session the recipe token, the signed summary `Recipe share` over the ingredient count (never localized), the recipe's title as the subject and the payload's JSON from a default `JSONEncoder`, encoded when the pairing verifies. The receive handler keeps the inbound rules: a 1 MiB size gate before decoding, the format and version check, the 512 KiB image drop, the review clamps, a 3 s per-sender rate limit (keyed by the raw fingerprint) and an eight-share queue. One renderer at the foot of the file (`sendState(for:)`, `statusMessage(for:)`, `diagnostic(for:)`, exhaustive with no `default`) turns the session's status and events into the frozen sentences the sheet renders verbatim; the receive handler's four lines are its own. Code-owned. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletConnections/FriendMintingReview.swift` | Pure view-free decision logic for the post-session keep-as-friend prompt: chooses the session-end review mode and filters roster candidates for eligibility against the trust vault at presentation time. Fernlet's rule, in `FernletConnections`; `ConnectView`, `DisposableCameraView` and `SessionPhotoReviewCoordinator` call it. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Moderation/ModerationBanStore.swift` | Tamper-resistant 30-day designer store-ban clock: reinstall-proof via a dedicated Keychain service and clock-tamper-proof via a `mach_continuous_time` countdown plus a wall-clock high-water ratchet. Reconciles in both directions: applies warranted bans with their salted-tag evidence, and lifts an active ban whose reporters positively withdrew enough of it. Its rows go through FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem` and its audit lines (`storeBan.*`) to `FernletAuditLog`; code-owned (a keychain service and a hash domain). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Moderation/ModerationContentHash.swift` | Stable content key an abuse report binds to: order-stable SHA-256 over a sanitized item's artwork (texture grid + slot), so relisting under a new id/name cannot evade a report. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Moderation/ModerationLedger.swift` | `@MainActor` append-only JSON sidecar of moderation report/retract rows keyed for idempotent de-dupe and supersede-by-`reporterSeq`; bounded, never synced, excluded from iCloud device backups. Never fails open (bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §4): an undecodable file is quarantined to `ModerationLedger.json.corrupt`, rows decode one by one, and an unreadable file blocks every save until a read succeeds. Its file is the one its caller states (no default root). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Moderation/ModerationReportRelay.swift` | One-hop report wire: `SignedModerationReport`/`ModerationReportPayload` models (with the 32-row `maxReports` cap) plus signing and verification of each row's Ed25519 signature, through ProximityKit's identity under `FernletFeatureSignatures.moderationReportV2`, the host-label twin of `FernletCryptoPurpose.Signature.moderationReportV2`, against the transport-verified sender key (no transitive relay — the Sybil defense). In CODEOWNERS. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Moderation/ModerationReportExchange.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.7**: the report relay's mesh wiring — the `.itemReport` handler on the mesh's payload token door, which keeps a vault-trusted, unblocked friend's own verified rows for the app, and the commit-time send of this device's own signed reports to a vault-trusted friend through the mesh's captured-slot send. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Wire/ActivityPayloads.swift` | Group Activities wire envelopes plus `schemaVersion`-gated join-token/roster-snapshot signing and verification (through ProximityKit's identity under the activities' host labels, `FernletFeatureSignatures`, twins of their FernletCrypto labels) and the `ActivityParamsHash` descriptor hash. In CODEOWNERS. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Wire/FeatureCanonicalBytes.swift` | The canonical signing bytes of Fernlet's mesh features: each feature's domain tag and `canonicalBytes(for:)` overload — the activity descriptor (whose params hash is taken over them), the join token, the roster snapshot and the moderation report row — written with ProximityKit's public `CanonicalByteWriter` under the four FernletCrypto labels; signing input only. Beside them `FernletFeatureSignatures` (public), the three host labels the join token, the roster snapshot and the report row are signed and verified under through ProximityKit's identity, each minted with `ProximityCryptographicPurpose.featureSignature(_:framing:)` (length-prefixed) from its registry twin's spelling (plan step A1.0). In CODEOWNERS. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Wire/MessagePayloads.swift` | `TempMessagePayload` wire value type for a single sealed live-session chat message (id/text/sentAt), frozen and parked: nothing emits or dispatches it since chat rides the routed store, and it stays decodable so an older peer's frame parks by name. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/Wire/RecipeSharePayloads.swift` | `ProximityRecipeSharePayload` wire envelope (`FernletSocial`'s since ProximityKit plan step A0.7.3, byte for byte) wrapping a `ProximitySharedRecipe` (`ProximitySharedRecipeKind`, a local `SharedRecipePayload` or a `SharedSavedRecipePayload` with its bounded decode) for in-person recipe sharing, plus the optional 512 KB `imageJPEGData` wire image (cap enforced at the receive door via `droppingOversizeImage()`), the review clamps, the share-notes / include-picture strips (the notes strip rewrites a multipart partition through `SharedRecipePayload.withoutSteps()`) and `PendingProximityRecipeShare`, a received share awaiting review. Code-owned. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletConnections/TrainerPayloads.swift` | `TrainerExportPayload` wire envelope carrying the opaque, size-bounded curated Trainer/Nutritionist export bundle; sealing-required so an unsealed send fails closed at verify. Payload vocabulary of Fernlet's coach channel, in `FernletConnections`; its wire cap is ProximityKit's `ProximityCoordinator.maxTrainerModeInboundBytes` (4 MiB) and its bundle cap half of that. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletConnections/CoachSessionTrustPolicy.swift` | `CoachSessionContract` (the written-down coach/trainee role split) and the coach trust policy — a coach is NOT a friend, so only an unrevoked `.trainer` vault record with `unknownModeToken == nil` auto-confirms; its coordinator's audits reach the vault converted, as the friend policy's do. Fernlet's coach-channel rules, in `FernletConnections`. **No production callers yet.** |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropService.swift` | Offline "away" hearts over the CloudKit public-DB dead-drop. All crypto happens here on the sealed side of the wall; the injected transport only ever sees rotating day tags plus ciphertext. Its `identity` has no default — the host passes the identity built from its namespace — and its `storage` defaults to `HeartDropStorageScope.production` (the app passes its own scope). Audits through `FernletAuditLog`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropSealer.swift` | The outer seal for heart drops: versioned wire form with a prekey id (all-zeros = sealed to the static key). Gates payload size *before* key agreement. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartPrekeyStore.swift` | One-time X25519 prekeys for forward-secret drops, plus the X3DH-style signed prekey. Private halves live in one keychain blob (`AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, never synchronizable) under `com.fernlet.heartdrop`, through FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem`. Its `Bundle`, `PrekeyEntry` and `SignedPrekey` are typealiases of the core wire type `ProximityPrekeyBundle` and its nested types. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropPeerBundleCache.swift` | Cached prekey bundles gossiped by friends plus per-friend consumed-prekey marking. A bundle is only ever stored from a verified, signed identity intro, keyed by the sender's full signing key. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropOutbox.swift` | Persisted sender-side queue for offline drops plus the durable receive dedup — two sidecars beside `HeartLedger.json`, deliberately outside the synced snapshot. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropSidecarKey.swift` | `HeartDropSidecarSeal`, the keychain-backed ChaChaPoly seal for the heart-drop sidecars at rest — the plaintext versions were a timestamped log of who the user sent affection to. One-way plaintext→sealed migration; `FSC2` required, `FSC1` refused by name and still classified. Its key row goes through FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropStorageScope.swift` | The storage identity of one device's heart-drop state — sidecar directory + the keychain service holding the seal key and prekey blob. One value because `wipeForDeleteAll()` destroys both, so isolating either alone isolates nothing. `production` is Fernlet's proximity root, `FernletConnections`' `FernletProximityStorage.productionDirectory` (`Application Support/Fernlet`, the path the stores have always used), + `com.fernlet.heartdrop`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/HeartDropSidecarFormatCensus.swift` | Read-only census of the heart-drop sidecar corpus: each of the four known files (outbox, peer bundles, dedup, the outbox's quarantine) classified by its marker bytes alone (`FSC2`, legacy `FSC1`, neither, empty, unreadable, absent), reading no keychain row and decoding nothing; a report is clean only on positive evidence. The app's DEBUG crypto-format census and Phase-3 gate readout read it. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/IdentityService+HeartDrop.swift` | The heart dead-drop's derivations as `IdentityService` extensions: `heartDropDayEpoch(at:)` (the UTC day), `heartDropPairSecret(with:)` (ProximityKit's `pairSecret(with:purpose:)` under `FernletFeaturePurposes.heartDropPairV1`, refusing a missing key-agreement key with `notProvisioned` first and a malformed friend key with `sealFailed` then) and `heartDropTag(pairSecret:dayEpoch:senderKeyAgreementPublicKey:)` (HMAC over `fernlet.heartdrop.day.v1`, the day and the sender's key, 16 bytes as hex), with `TagCounterBytes`, the big-endian counter bytes the presence tag uses too. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshStorageScopes+HeartDrop.swift` | `keychainService(besideHeartDrop:in:)` on ProximityKit's `MeshSessionStorageScope` and `MeshRoutedStorageScope`: the production heart-drop service maps to the namespace's seal-key service, any other to `<service>.mesh-session` or `<service>.mesh-routed`, which is how `FernletStore` isolates a test store's mesh keys. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshSessionHearts.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.10**: `MeshSessionHearts`, the in-session hearts (the composition's `hearts`) — the routed heart to one recipient (`sendSessionHeart(to:)`), its `SessionHeartState` and `SessionHeartFailure` with their auto-clear, the in-flight claims, the two reachability questions the camera's heart button asks (`canSendSessionHeart(toFingerprint:)`, the engine's addressability query, and `hasLiveHeartSlot(forFingerprint:)`), and the heart row's ledger judge (a private adapter: hearts on and the ledger loaded as its readiness, a trusted, unblocked author as its admission, the heart ledger's commit as its judgement). In CODEOWNERS (the `HeartSharing/` row). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshRoutedHeartBody.swift` | P6 item 6: `MeshRoutedHeartHeader`/`MeshRoutedHeartBody`, the header-only plaintext of a routed heart (so its cap formula has a ZERO payload term), refusing a non-empty remainder, with a day-key shape check and the 128-byte name bound refused on the wire; its body id is the gift id. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshRoutedHeartAck.swift` | P5 item 4: `MeshRoutedHeartAck`, a routed heart's ledger judgement as PROOF — the heart ledger's commit outcome and its `MeshHeartLedgerProof` for one gift; the heart judge's proof gate, never the evidence, which the engine mints from the judge's answer. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshHeartCommit.swift` | P4 item 7, second half: `MeshHeartCommit`, the heart receipt's commit of a merged batch through the existing `ProximityHeartLedger` (its id-dedup and its 5-minute cooldown arbitrate), and `MeshHeartCommitOutcome`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletSocial/HeartSharing/MeshMergedHeart.swift` | P4 item 7: `MeshMergedHeart`, the heart as the content merge sees it — union by `(author, gift ID)` over ProximityKit's generic union; no receipt field. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/VerifyQRViews.swift` | `QRCodeRenderer` plus the verify-QR display/scan sheets — renders this device's signed `fernlet://verify` URL for the ceremony above. |

## Private Media Stores

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PrivateMediaStore.swift` | Disk-backed mesh photo index with **AES-256-GCM at-rest encryption** of image/thumbnail bytes and of the index itself (`MeshPhotoCache.sealed` under the friend-wall media key; a legacy plaintext `MeshPhotoCache.json` is read once, resealed, then deleted), thumbnail generation, hydration, FIFO eviction (1000 cap / 900 warn), and orphan cleanup. (Formerly `MeshPhotoCacheStore`.) `maxIncomingPhotoBytes` is `public` since **P6 item 3** so the routed mesh path derives its per-type ciphertext cap from this one plaintext bound instead of restating 10 MB. Since 2026-09-30: an index file that EXISTS but cannot be read loads `.deferred` (never as an empty wall a later save would sweep), and `commitKept(_:onto:) -> WallKeepResult` is the per-photo keep commit a review answer uses — it reports only photos whose sealed bytes landed and the committed index names, never evicts a photo it is keeping, removes its own files when the index write fails, and returns the committed wall (`committedWall`) the owner replaces its mirror with. Every index write cuts session participant lists to the wire decode bound and decodes its bytes back before sealing, so an index always reads back. File mechanics live in `FriendPhotoCorpusFiles`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/FriendPhotoCorpusFiles.swift` | Internal `FriendPhotoCorpusFiles`: the file half both friend-photo corpora share — sealed full-size write reporting whether it landed (`ImageWrite`), the decompression-bomb checks, ImageIO thumbnails, sealed reads (legacy plaintext only where allowed), and the orphan sweep. The wall passes its names/purposes with legacy plaintext allowed; the pending corpus passes its own, born sealed. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PendingSessionPhotoStore.swift` | `PendingSessionPhotoStore` + `PendingSessionPhotoIndex` / `HeldSessionPhoto` / `HeldPhotoKey` / `AnsweredSessionPhoto`: the sealed corpus of session photos nobody has chosen yet (`PendingSessionPhotos/`, excluded from backup, device-bound key, three `privatePendingSessionPhoto*V1` purposes). Identity by origin + item id with a separate local id for files; hold refused past 200 (never an eviction); answers are one index write with 24 h tombstones (cap 1024); no timer ever removes a held photo; deferred on an unreadable/keyless/newer-format index, purged only on an AEAD failure; `answering(_:in:now:)` is the pure successor an owner adopts when an answer's write fails; `readImage(for:)` → `HeldImageRead` tells a photo that is GONE (missing, or not opening under a present key) from one that cannot be read RIGHT NOW (no key, a file read that failed), so a keep never deletes a ticked photo on a transient failure; every index write reads back by construction (participant lists cut to the decode bound, bytes decoded before sealing); orphan sweep on every clean load; keyless `purgeAll()` for delete-all. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/PrivateMediaKeyStore.swift` | `PrivateMediaKeyProviding` + the keychain-backed AES key provider, split per `Role`: `.friendWall` = the original backup-restorable `AfterFirstUnlock` row (`…contentKey`, unchanged), `.ownPhotos` = a second row (`…ownContentKey`) for the user's own meal/recipe/progress media, whose device binding is gated by `OwnPhotoKeyBinder`, and (2026-09-30) `.pendingSessionPhotos` = a third row (`…pendingContentKey`) for the pending session-photo corpus, born `AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`. All three live under `com.fernlet.private-media`, the service the duress wipe sweeps. Fails closed (no mint) when the keychain read is *unreadable* rather than definitively absent. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/OwnPhotoCorpusLayout.swift` | The one home of the own photo corpora's on-disk names (`OwnPhotoCorpusLayout`) and the location set that walks them (`OwnPhotoSealedLocations`). Was `OwnPhotoKeyMigration.swift` and also held `OwnPhotoKeyMigrator` + `OwnPhotoMigrationLatch`; both were retired at the close of the crypto standardization round (owner decision 2026-08-29) once Phase 3's deletion of the unmarked at-rest read left the re-seal pass with no reachable input, and `MediaAtRestFormatMigrationLatch` inherited its half of `OwnPhotoKeyBinder`'s binding gate. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/OwnPhotoKeyBinding.swift` | The own-photo key's device-binding gate (Phase 5, step 5c): `OwnPhotoKeyBinder` (refuses unless the migration latch is set AND the user has a cross-device route, then re-binds the row in place with `SecItemUpdate` — never delete-then-add, which would open a window with no own-photos key at all), `OwnPhotoDeviceBindingConsent` (the recorded, one-way user acceptance for the escrow-off branch), and `OwnPhotoKeyBindingOutcome`. Also the keychain accessors that read and set the row's accessibility class. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/MediaAtRestCrypto.swift` | Shared AES-256-GCM at-rest helpers on `PrivateMediaKeyProviding` (`gcmSeal`, `gcmOpen`, `sealAndWrite` — the last writes nothing at all on a nil key or seal failure, so plaintext never reaches disk). The one home of the seal / open / seal-then-write idiom `PrivateMediaStore`, `MealPhotoStore`, and `ProgressPhotoStore` each hand-rolled; deliberately policy-free, so each store keeps its own fail-closed decision (and its legacy-plaintext branch) at the call site. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/FriendPhotoImageHelpers.swift` | `UIImage` resizing helper for friend-photo sharing. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/MealPhotoStore.swift` | `MealPhotoStore`: on-device AES-256-GCM-sealed store for meal and other private photos with bounded downscale. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/PrivateMediaStore/ProgressPhotoStore.swift` | `ProgressPhotoStore`: sealed gym progress-photo timeline over `MealPhotoStore` bytes plus a GCM-sealed dated index; `mergeRestoredIndexPayload` joins an escrow restore's rows to a live timeline (bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §2). |

## Widgets, Live Activities, And App Intents

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/FernletWidgetsBundle.swift` | `@main` `WidgetBundle` registering the companion widget (mood glyph + water, systemSmall/systemMedium/Lock Screen; emotion faces on the Home Screen families only, a timeline entry at every emotion transition) and the guided-workout and cooking Live Activities. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WidgetSharedModels.swift` | Deliberately duplicated app-group models (`WidgetSnapshot`, `PendingWidgetAction`, `WidgetCompanionState`, `WidgetCompanionEmotion`, `WidgetEmotionTimeline`, day-key/JSON helpers) so the standalone widget extension avoids linking FernletKit. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WaterPlusOneIntent.swift` | `AppIntent` behind the companion widget's interactive "+1 water" button; queues a pending action and reloads the widget timeline. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutLiveActivity.swift` | Widget-target `Widget` rendering the Lock Screen card and Dynamic Island for the guided-workout rest-timer Live Activity: rest and hold countdowns, "−15 s" · "Skip rest" · "+15 s" while resting, and the set's weight, range and last time's numbers (display only; compact and minimal unchanged). |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutActivityLoadText.swift` | `WorkoutActivityLoadText` — the drawn and spoken words for a set's load on the Live Activity ("105 lb", "95–110 lb", "Last time: 3 × 8 @ 100 lb"; "Suggested weight 105 pounds, …"), formatted in the reader's locale. Compiled into both targets; the runner's `GuidedLoadFormat` formats its weights through it. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutActivityZoneText.swift` | `WorkoutActivityZoneText` — the drawn and spoken words for a cardio segment's heart-rate cue ("118 bpm · aim 110–125 · in range"; "Heart rate 118, aiming for 110 to 125, in range") and the short talk test when there is no live reading. Compiled into both targets; the runner's readout uses it too. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/WorkoutActivityAttributes.swift` | `ActivityAttributes`/`ContentState` contract for the guided-workout Live Activity, compiled into both the app and widget targets. `ContentState.load` carries the set's weight, range and last time's numbers as optional numbers and unit tokens (an older payload still decodes). |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutRunState.swift` | Codable/Hashable value type for an in-progress guided workout, mirrored into the app-group container so Lock Screen intents can advance it. Carries each lift's last-time numbers (baked at start) and `adjustRest(by:now:)` — ±15 s that never leaves under 5 s or passes 10 min. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/AppGroupRunStateStore.swift` | `AppGroupRunStateStore` + `AppGroupRunStatePersistable` — the generic `NSFileCoordinator`-guarded app-group reader/writer behind both run-state files (ISO-8601 + sorted-keys codecs, atomic protected writes, nil-tolerant reads that treat a missing/corrupt/still-protected file as "no active run", and the `updatedAt` re-stamp the app's reconcile ages out on). The single home of the plumbing `GuidedWorkoutRunStateStore` and `CookingRunStateStore` each carried; self-contained (literal app-group id) so it compiles identically into both targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutRunStateStore.swift` | The guided-workout specialization of that store — now a `typealias` for `AppGroupRunStateStore<GuidedWorkoutRunState>`; the persistence seam between the app's in-app transitions and the Lock Screen intent runner. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutActivityBridge.swift` | Shared bridge (app + widget targets) that syncs a `GuidedWorkoutRunState` onto the live workout Activity, updating or ending it. Remains the documented seam, now delegating the loop to `LiveActivityReflector`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/LiveActivityRunReflecting.swift` | `LiveActivityRunReflectable` + `LiveActivityReflector` — the one generic update/end engine (update while the run is live, end on terminal) that `GuidedWorkoutActivityBridge` and `CookingActivityBridge` delegate to instead of each carrying the loop. Every call is parameterized by its own `ActivityAttributes` type, so ending one activity kind can never enumerate the other's instances. Compiled into both the app and widget targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/GuidedWorkoutLiveActivityIntents.swift` | `LiveActivityIntent`s for the guided-workout Live Activity's "Done set", "Skip rest", "−15 s" and "+15 s" Lock Screen buttons. After a durable write they post `.guidedWorkoutRunAdvancedByIntent` (the cooking twin's pattern), which `FernletStore` observes to reconcile the in-app run at once. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WidgetBridge.swift` | `WidgetSnapshot` + bridge — app side of the FernletWidgets app-group JSON bridge: outbound benign snapshot and inbound pending widget actions (deliberately S3-walled twin types). **P10 item 4** adds the CONTENT/METADATA split the background refresh's "reload timelines only on change" rests on: `WidgetSnapshot.contentEquals(_:)` compares the six fields that decide what the widget DRAWS and ignores `computedAt`, which is stamped `Date()` at every construction — so the synthesised `==` is false between two snapshots of an unchanged day and a diff built on it would reload the timelines forever. The doc comment carries the per-field table; `WidgetSnapshotContentEqualityTests` pins it, including a `Mirror` field-count pin that reds when a field is added without a classification. `WidgetSnapshotMirror.publishIfContentChanged(_:)` is the handler's publish door — it always WRITES (the stamp moves) and reloads only on a content change, answering `WidgetSnapshotPublication`. `publish(_:)` is untouched and still reloads unconditionally: that scoping is the foreground's, every one of whose callers is a change by construction, and a cell pins it. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshIdentifier.swift` | **P10 item 2** (plan §16.4, §17.2): `CompanionRefresh` — the companion background refresh's namespace, holding its one frozen `BGAppRefreshTask` identifier `MBO.Fernlet.companion-refresh` and nothing else (item 3 registers it and adds the `Info.plist` entries; item 4 is the handler). `nonisolated`, because the app target defaults to MainActor isolation and an identifier that needs an `await` to read is one the registration site cannot use — the same reason `MeshContinuationTaskHost.identifierPrefix` is spelled that way. It exists in its own directory on purpose: `App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/` is the unit `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests` walks, so items 3 and 4 are inside §16.4's import wall from the moment their files land, and this literal is that wall's positive needle — an emptied directory reds it instead of passing vacuously. Writes nothing, so the wipe wall is owed no disposition row. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshScheduling.swift` | **P10 item 3** (plan §17.2, §27.1): the companion refresh's OWN `BackgroundTasks` seam — `CompanionRefreshScheduling` (register / submit), `CompanionRefreshTaskHandle` (expiration handler, complete) and the `CompanionRefreshRequest` value, plus the production conformers `SystemCompanionRefreshScheduler` and `SystemCompanionRefreshTaskHandle`. A SECOND seam rather than a widened `MeshContinuationScheduling`, deliberately: two tasks through one protocol is how a fake stops modelling either, and the mesh's members (progress, a two-sentence card, four ending reasons) have no meaning for an app refresh. Three places the mesh's shape did NOT transfer, each with its reason in the header: no pre-register cancel (the refresh identifier is process-stable, so a request from a previous launch is exactly what this one wants delivered — cancelling would break the chain every cold start, silently), no pre-submit cancel (the pending request IS the fallback when a submission throws), and no `strategy` / `progress` / `updateTitle` (`BGAppRefreshTaskRequest` has none of them; `earliestBeginDate` is the only thing there is to ask for). It is the app's only file that names `BGAppRefreshTask`, which `MeshContinuationTaskHostTests` cell (a) pins in both directions alongside the mesh's `BGContinuedProcessingTask`. The conformers are the untested remainder and are item 9's device row — including §27.2's open measurement of what a Simulator does with an app-refresh submission at all. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshCoordinator.swift` | **P10 item 3**: `CompanionRefreshCoordinator` — the process-global (`.shared`, an immutable `static let` in `FernletStoreAccess.shared`'s idiom) that owns the companion `BGAppRefreshTask`: one registration at launch, the two submission triggers, and exactly-once completion of whatever the system delivers. **The schedule policy is at handle + background, never on a timer** (plan §26.3/§27.3): trigger (a) is the tail of every handler run, which submits the successor BEFORE it completes the task, and trigger (b) is the `.background` scene edge — there is no third, and no clock at all (no `Timer`, no `DispatchQueue`, no `Task.sleep`; the app owns exactly one timer and it is `ProximitySessionPoller`'s). **The edge asks only when nothing is pending**, held in an in-memory `pendingRequest` slot: `BGTaskScheduler` REPLACES a same-identifier request, so an unconditional edge submission slid the floor fifteen minutes further out on every switch away and a person who opened the app more often than that would never be delivered one. A delivery empties the slot, a refusal leaves it empty, and the tail is unconditional because it runs after a delivery; the accepted cost of an in-memory slot is one slide per process launch. The R2 cap (`maxEdgeSubmissionsPerLaunch`) bounds the EDGE alone — the tail is exempt, because a never-reset lifetime budget shared with it would end the chain permanently on a process iOS keeps suspended for days. `earliestBeginInterval` is 15 minutes: a floor the app states, not a cadence it gets, and a constant so a soak can move it with a measurement. **Item 3's handler is a placeholder by design** — adopt, submit the successor, complete once — so §17.2's seven steps land in item 4 as an insertion between two existing lines rather than a rewrite, and so §16.4's import wall and the exactly-once contract are both already true of the directory before any work runs inside it. Writes nothing: no `UserDefaults`, no "last refreshed at", nothing on disk, so the wipe wall is owed no disposition row. Speaks the seam's protocols and never `BackgroundTasks` itself. **2026-09-21 (item 2c):** the edge budget is charged AFTER `submitNext(trigger:)` reports the ask reached the seam, so a launch whose registration was refused spends none of it and its audit trail keeps naming the refusal instead of the cap (plan §17.2.3 finding 3); `edgeSubmissions <= submissions` is now an invariant. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshPipeline.swift` | **P10 item 4** (plan §17.2, §27.3): the companion refresh HANDLER, written as a pipeline over VALUES — `CompanionRefreshStep` (the trace), `CompanionRefreshOutcome` (what the system is told), `CompanionRefreshRun` (trace + verdict + the two facts worth reporting), `CompanionRefreshSteps` (the five post-acquisition steps as `@MainActor` closures) and `CompanionRefreshPipeline` itself. Closures rather than a `FernletStore` parameter because a handler that named the store could be driven in a unit test only by BUILDING one, which is the single thing §17.2 forbids outright; acquire is two-phase (it returns the remaining steps rather than a store) so the store appears in no type here at all. The order is the claim: acquire → inspect the widget action queue → roll the day → recompute the companion → publish through the diff → reload only on a change. A queued widget action ends the run at the inspection with a named outcome and NO publish (decision D-10.4.2: the widget's own "+1 water" App Intent has already written a provisional count into the same file, and republishing the app's lower one would make the count go backwards; §17.2 forbids draining here, and the roll is skipped with the publish because a warm process's roll publishes on its own). Cancellation is checked at the two places it can land — before the acquisition and immediately after it, the pipeline's one suspension point — and nowhere else, because the tail is synchronous main-actor work a cancellation cannot cut into. Never throws and never traps: a background handler that threw would leave the coordinator owing the system a completion, so every failure is a value. Writes nothing and reads no clock; it builds no store of its own, and the acquisition it calls builds the PROCESS's first one on a cold wake, which is what §17.2 allows and what the item-4 verify corrected three places for saying otherwise. **Item 4's verify fixes added the two refusals and the roll's own reload.** The queue skip now yields to a new day (D-10.4.8: one undrained row used to suspend every background publish until the next foreground, and across midnight a snapshot left on yesterday's `dateKey` makes `WidgetDayGate` blank the companion — a widget a tap behind beats a blank one, reported as `publishedDespitePendingActions`). A scoring adjustment switched on without its bridge ends the run at a new `inspectScoringContext` step (D-10.4.6: the bridges are attached only by `ContentView`, so a COLD wake recomputes a score missing the period and stress adjustments, and the handler refuses to publish a number the app itself disagrees with — `scoringContextUnavailable`). And the snapshot file is read either side of the roll, because `refreshCurrentDayIfNeeded()` publishes through the store's own mirror on an advance and that write used to satisfy the handler's diff before it ran, leaving `dayAdvanced` true with no reload in the trace on the most consequential refresh of the day. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/CompanionRefresh/CompanionRefreshWiring.swift` | **P10 item 4**: the ONE place the pipeline's abstract steps are bound to the real app, and therefore the only file in the refresh directory that names anything of the app's. Eight one-expression bindings — `FernletStoreAccess.shared.load()` (the sanctioned acquisition, which refuses before first unlock, hands back the CACHED store, and on a COLD wake builds the process's first one inside the grant), `hasUndrainedWidgetActions`, `publishedWidgetSnapshotIsForCurrentDay`, `hasCompleteScoringContext`, `publishedWidgetSnapshot()`, `refreshCurrentDayIfNeeded()`, `companionState.rawValue`, `currentWidgetSnapshot()` and `ensureWidgetSnapshotMirror().publishIfContentChanged(_:)` — each on `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests`' PERMITTED list, so a reviewer can read the body in one screen and decide whether the handler reaches anything it must not. `steps(for:)` is split out of `productionPipeline()` so those five expressions are reachable from a test over an ordinary test store rather than only through the process-global cache. The header records what the day roll and the recompute were TRACED to touch, since the wall cannot see inside them: repository reads and writes only — no HealthKit call, no radio, no forced CloudKit sync (the Core Data stack's own mirroring to the user's private database is ambient, not a sync this handler asks for), and `day.healthContext` is values HealthKit wrote into the diary on an earlier foreground. Imports `FernletDomainModel` for one member, `CompanionState.rawValue`, which member-import visibility makes explicit. Item 4's verify fixes write down what ensuring the mirror COSTS (decision D-10.4.7, accepted): in a process woken purely for background work the mirror outlives the refresh, so any later save there — a Siri water intent — now reloads the widget unconditionally, which was impossible before and is exactly what the foreground already does. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/WorkoutLiveActivityController.swift` | `WorkoutLiveActivityController` — app-side requester for the guided-workout Live Activity (starts one, ends any stale one; degrades silently when Live Activities are disabled). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/LiveActivityStarter.swift` | `LiveActivityStarter.start` — the shared generic requester behind `WorkoutLiveActivityController` and `CookingLiveActivityController`: ends any already-live activity of the same attributes type, then requests one, and no-ops when the user hasn't enabled Live Activities (request failures degrade silently). App-target only and deliberately outside the dual-membership set, because only the app process can start an activity — unlike the update/end engine (`LiveActivityRunReflecting.swift`), which the Lock Screen intents also need. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletNavigation.swift` | App navigation enums (`FernletTab`, `FernletSheet`) — split out of the design system when it moved into the FernletUI package target (`FernletSheet` references app-resident `FirstAidTool`). Also the re-tap-the-active-tab router: `TabReselectAction` (pop to the tab's main page when something is pushed — after the shared discard alert when a pushed page holds typed input — else scroll to top), `TabReselectModifier` / `.tabReselect(...)`, attached outside each tab page's `NavigationStack`, and the draft half: `TabDraftRegistry` (weak `TabDraftLease`s, injected into the tab's stack) fed by `TabReselectDraftModifier` / `.tabReselectDraft(isDirty:)` on pushed editors. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletAppIntents.swift` | App Intents — `LogWaterIntent` (background app-group queue append, no app launch), `LogMealIntent` / `OpenJournalIntent` (open the app to the matching sheet via a persisted deep-link). |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/FernletShortcuts.swift` | `FernletShortcuts` (`AppShortcutsProvider`) — surfaces the log-water/log-meal/open-journal App Intents to Siri and Spotlight with natural phrases. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExchangeIntentService.swift` | `ExchangeIntentService` — the main-process dependency behind the recipe / workout-plan exchange App Intents, plus the entities those intents speak in (`RecipeEntity`, `PlannedWorkoutEntity`, their `EntityQuery`s, `WorkoutPlanImportPreview`, the duplicate/collision policy enums, `ExchangeIntentLimits`, and `ExchangeIntentServiceError`). It creates no repositories and owns no store of its own: the scene loader (via `loadStoreForUI`) and every background intent coalesce through `FernletStoreAccess`. A workout-plan preview carries a calendar-revision fingerprint and an expiry, so an approved plan cannot be applied against a calendar that changed underneath it, and both import paths consult `ExchangeImportLedger` so a retried automation replays its result instead of importing a second copy. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingActivityAttributes.swift` | ActivityKit contract for the cooking Live Activity (recipe name + per-step `ContentState`), compiled into both targets. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingLiveActivity.swift` | The cooking Live Activity widget: Lock Screen card plus every Dynamic Island presentation. Registered in `FernletWidgetsBundle` alongside the workout activity. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingActivityBridge.swift` | The single seam reflecting a `CookingRunState` onto the live activity — shared by the in-app walker and the Lock Screen intents. Delegates its loop to `LiveActivityReflector`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingLiveActivityIntents.swift` | `LiveActivityIntent`s for the cooking activity's "Next"/repeat-step buttons and the matching Siri phrases; `CookingIntentRunner` performs them in the app's process and posts the notification `FernletStore` reconciles on. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingRunState.swift` | A cooking session as a flat value that survives process death in the app group, carrying the full ordered `steps` so a cold-launched intent can render the next step with no live app state. Each step may carry its multipart `partName` (optional key); `currentPart` and the Live Activity text ("Lemon dressing · …") read it. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletWidgets/CookingRunStateStore.swift` | Coordinated app-group reader/writer for `CookingRunState.json` — the persistence seam between `FernletStore` and the Lock Screen/Siri intent runner. |

## Messages Extension

The only UIKit target Fernlet ships, and a two-process boundary: the appex reads an App Group
catalog the app publishes and writes hand-off inboxes the app drains. It links exactly one
FernletKit product (`FernletExchange`) and no repository — `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests` scans
the whole directory to keep it that way.

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesViewController.swift` | `FernletMessagesViewController` — the `MSMessagesAppViewController` composer and received-item viewer. Reads only the bounded App Group catalog, and independently decodes every received envelope rather than trusting the visible message card. Draws its own 1200×630 card artwork locally, so Messages never fetches or exposes a private food photo. Persists nothing of its own since 2026-09-23 (the last-picked-recipe key it kept in its own `UserDefaults.standard`, which the wipe could not reach, was removed). Since 2026-09-30 an opened card is drawn from its own URL alone — `FernletMessagesReceivedItem.resolve` decides the screen, and only the composer reads the catalog (`viewDidLoad` shows an opening state instead of building the composer, and only while no activation has decided yet, so a card drawn before the view loaded is never reset) — held by `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests.aReceivedCardIsDrawnWithoutTheComposersCatalog`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCopy.swift` | `FernletMessagesCopy` — the target's copy vault (the `FernletUICopy`/`FernletLockCopy` model). Every sentence the extension shows, keyed and catalogued, with computed members so the lookup happens under the locale in force at use time — except what a recipe card carries, which moved to `FernletMessagesCardCopy` on 2026-09-30. NO `bundle:` argument: an appex's `Bundle.main` is its own. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCard.swift` | `FernletMessagesCard` + `FernletMessagesPalette` (2026-09-30) — the ONE builder of a Fernlet Messages card: the envelope's `messageURL()`, the recipe template layout (name and counts read off the envelope's validated card), the 1200×630 artwork and the summary line. ALSO a member of the Fernlet app target (a membership exception on this synchronized folder), so the composer's inserted card and the app's "Send in Messages" card are the same bytes. Imports only the extension's four permitted modules. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/FernletMessagesCardCopy.swift` | `FernletMessagesCardCopy` (2026-09-30) — the eight strings a recipe card carries (captions, "Opens in Fernlet on iPhone", the wordmark, the summary line, the three plural-ruled counts), keys unchanged from `FernletMessagesCopy`. Compiled into the app too, where `catalog` is the embedded extension bundle (`PlugIns/FernletMessagesExtension.appex`), so both processes read the extension's catalog and the keys and the counts' plural blocks live there only; `Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh` keeps them out of the app catalog's harvest. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/Localizable.xcstrings` | The target's string catalog, added 2026-08-27 — the target shipped a round without one, so `String(localized:)` there would have harvested nothing. Synced by `Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh`; the four count keys carry hand-authored plural variations. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/Assets.xcassets` | The `iMessage App Icon` set App Store Connect requires of an iMessage app (all twelve Xcode slots, including the 1024×768 Messages App Store image), wired through `ASSETCATALOG_COMPILER_APPICON_NAME`. Real art since 2026-09-24 (owner-approved; it replaced the 2026-09-23 placeholder): the Home Screen icon's fern sprig, turned onto the 4:3 diagonal and fitted inside the superellipse the Messages "+" menu clips it to. Rendered by `Scripts/render-imessage-icon.py`, which is the set's source of truth — never hand-edit the PNGs. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy` | The appex's own privacy manifest (App Store Connect checks each executable against its own bundle's manifest). Declares no tracking and no required-reason API — held to the code compiled into the binary by `MessagesExtensionBoundaryTests`, and pinned for tracking flags by `NoTrackingBoundaryTests`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletMessagesExtension/MessageTransportProbe.swift` | Phase-0 physical-device probe proving `MSMessage.url` can carry opaque bytes. DEAD CODE — zero call sites anywhere, including tests. Delete it or wire it up. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletExchange.swift` | The exchange core: `RecipeExchangePacket` (format version 1 for a one-part recipe — byte-for-byte what every earlier build wrote — and version 2 for a recipe made in parts, whose `contentHash` pre-image carries its version and the parts; each version may carry only its own shape), `WorkoutPlanExchangePacket`, `ExchangeCardMetadata`, `ExchangeLimits`, and `ExchangeMessageEnvelope` — the `data:` URL payload carried by `MSMessage.url`, written as version 2 (the packet's raw JSON, deflated and base64url-encoded once — see `ExchangeMessageWireV2.swift`) and read in both versions; the version-1 envelope still re-derives the card and requires equality, which is what makes a tampered visible card unusable. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/ExchangeMessageWireV2.swift` | `ExchangeMessageWireV2` — the version-2 Messages wire (2026-09-24): the four-byte frame (magic `F`, version 2, the document's `UInt16` length) around a raw-DEFLATE JSON document, the strict unpadded-base64url codec, and the bounded inflate — the declared length is checked against `ExchangeLimits.maxMessageDocumentBytes` before a byte is inflated, becomes the inflater's hard ceiling, and must match exactly. `OutputFilter` on both sides, so no unsafe pointer and no Power-of-10 allowlist entry. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesCatalog.swift` | `FernletMessagesCatalog` — the versioned App Group document the app publishes for the extension: prebuilt packets plus picker metadata, capped at 100 recipes / 100 workouts / 1 MiB. Also `FernletMessagesRecipePicker` / `FernletMessagesWorkoutPicker` (the compact/search rules) and `FernletMessagesCatalogFileStore`. Bounded by construction: browsing it can never widen into repository access. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesInbox.swift` | `FernletMessagesInboxStore` and `FernletMessagesInboxLink` — the recipe hand-off inbox. The extension enqueues an opaque record and opens a deep link; the app decodes and presents it for review. Records expire and the queue refuses overflow, so a post-wipe message cannot reopen old content. Cleared by `FernletStore.clearMessagesImportInboxes` — see [PrivacyWipeCoverage.md](PrivacyWipeCoverage.md). |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesWorkoutInbox.swift` | `FernletMessagesWorkoutInboxStore` / `FernletMessagesWorkoutInboxRecord` — the workout-plan twin of the recipe inbox, carrying the sender's suggested start day for revalidation against the recipient's calendar. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/FernletExchange/FernletMessagesReceivedItem.swift` | `FernletMessagesReceivedItem` (2026-09-30) — what the Messages extension shows for a card someone opened, decided from the card's URL alone: `.recipe`, `.workoutPlan` (already the review-inbox record it will be handed on as) or `.invalid`. Total and non-throwing, so no input yields an empty screen; reads no file, so opening a card never touches the App Group catalog. Moved out of the view controller so `MessagesReceivedItemTests` can pin it. |
| `Fernlet/App/Fernlet/ExchangeImportLedger.swift` | `ExchangeImportLedger` — the app-side replay ledger keyed on packet ID + content hash, so the same shared item forwarded twice is recognized as one import rather than duplicated. |

## Share Extension

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletShareExtension/ShareViewController.swift` | Share-extension entry `UIViewController` that extracts the shared URL and enqueues it via `SharedRecipeImportQueueWriter`. |
| `Fernlet/App/FernletShareExtension/SharedRecipeImportQueueWriter.swift` | App-group JSON queue writer that records shared recipe URLs (`SharedRecipeImportRecord`) for the app to import later. A deliberate hand-copied twin of `AppServices/SharedRecipeImportQueue.swift` (the extension links no FernletKit products); field list, coder config, container fallback chain, and `NSFileCoordinator` coordination must stay in sync — enforced by `SharedRecipeImportQueueMirrorTests`. |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/Sources/AppServices/SharedRecipeImportQueue.swift` | App-group-backed queue of shared recipe-URL import records (`SharedRecipeImportRecord` with attempt/error tracking) handed off from the share extension for later import. `enqueueBudgetDeferred(_:now:dayKey:)` also queues a PASTED link refused for today's AI budget, stamped deferred for today, so the paste screen's "I'll try this recipe again tomorrow" is kept. |

## Tests

Unit tests live in one target here, built strict by `Scripts/spm-wall-check.sh` and run through `Fernlet.xcworkspace`: the app-hosted `FernletTests` (`Tests/FernletTests`, the two tables below). ProximityKit's own package test target, `ProximityKitTests`, which lived under `FernletKit/Tests` from ProximityKit plan step A0.8, left with the package at A1.3 (its subsection below). `Tests/suite-inventory.tsv` holds every suite a CI line names to its file and the lines that run it.

### Unit Tests

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletTests.swift` | Core app/domain tests. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/Phase3GateReadoutTests.swift` | The Phase 3 gate readout's fold, driven as pure functions over hand-built fixtures: every way a row could manufacture a pass (an empty or truncated corpus, a stale keyed witness, a converting media pass, a manifest behind the device high-water mark, an untaken body probe, an invalid run scheme, a duress session), the media residue arithmetic and label mapping, and the redaction contract on BOTH export routes. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/Phase3ReadoutSessionTests.swift` | `Phase3ReadoutSession`'s stateful half — the seven-step checklist's derived done-states, the scan and session fences, the probe cap's keep-probe-#1 eviction, and `clear()` — plus the store-side `clearPhase3Evidence()` / `clearSealedColumnPassWitness()` that stand between a wipe (or a duress engage) and a manufactured discharge. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletUITests/Phase3GateReadoutUITests.swift` | The navigation smoke test for the Phase 3 gate readout: Settings → Connection log → Developer tools → the readout, asserting the page actually RENDERS. It exists because the view shipped reading its store from an environment nothing populates, which trapped on the push and which no unit test could have caught. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletPersistenceTests.swift` | Persistence behavior tests. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletLockTests.swift` | Lock-related integration or behavior tests. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletLockCryptoTests.swift` | Lock crypto tests: `FernletLockCrypto`'s scrypt passcode derivation, content-key wrapping, and verifier digest, plus the column-key HKDF derivation — which now lives in `ColumnCrypto` (FernletCrypto), characterized here via `@testable import FernletCrypto` including a pinned known-answer vector. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletLockServiceTests.swift` | Lock service tests plus local fake providers and harnesses; `CountedPasscodeDoorTests` (DPA-76) fails each door a typed passcode reaches four times and proves the ladder counts and locks out at every one, the duress-setup equality refusal is no oracle, and a mistype scrubs a resident key. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/ColumnCryptoDeviceBindingTests.swift` | Pins the device-bound sealed-column **v3** format: round trip, cross-install refusal, and — since the crypto standardization round's Phase 3 — the two retired generations as REFUSALS BY NAME rather than as compatibility (`SealedColumnOpenError.retiredFormat(_:)`, kept distinct from `emptyBlob` and `installBindingMissing`), the write side pinned CLOSED (`SealedColumnStrictSealError.bindingUnavailable` where it used to fall open), the retryable keychain-outage split (`DeviceBindingID.ReadError`, never an authentication failure), and the one resolution the round gave up — `aCollidedLegacyBlobCanNoLongerBeResolvedByOpen`, the ~1-in-256 blob whose first nonce byte collides with a marker. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SecureEnclaveWrapTests.swift` | Secure-Enclave content-key wrap tests: round trip, key-death on delete, lock-service maintain/repair without ever blocking unlock, reset removing the enclave key; both SE-present and SE-absent branches. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/KeyCustodyBoundaryTests.swift` | Key-custody tripwire: reads real keychain rows' accessibility/synchronizable attributes back per store, plus grep-walls confining `synchronizable: true` to `SealedBackupEscrowKey.swift` and bare non-ThisDeviceOnly accessibility classes to it and `PrivateMediaKeyStore.swift`, and the construction wall `everyShippingIdentityIsBuiltByItsHostsDoor`: over lexed shipping code (`SwiftSourceLexer`), an `IdentityService` is constructed only in the app's `SealedBackupEscrowKey.swift` (its factory, which carries the escrow; ProximityKit's host door has no default and builds none), a construction being a call of the type or of its metatype, or an implicit `.init(`, `Self(`, `Self.init(` or a metatype value's `.init(` under one of the identity's three first labels (`namespace:`, `keychainService:`, `provisioningParticipant:`), and also a `self.init(` delegation under one of them in code that names the identity, so a new convenience initializer or static factory fails it; its two matchers fixtured against nine constructions and eleven neighbours (since A1.1.20 a `super.init(keychainService:)`, `super` excluded beside `self`), two delegations and three neighbours; and a behavioural cell, `theStoresManagersHoldIdentitiesCarryingTheEscrowKey`: the app's real store (`makeTestStore()`) hands its mesh, recipe-share and presence managers, built with no identity, identities whose provisioning participant is a `SealedBackupEscrowKey` (one per identity, read off each manager by reflection, the recipe-share manager's through its pair session), and its host door answers the same. **Since ProximityKit plan step A1.1.5:** `proximityKitsKeychainFunnelReachesTheSystemKeychain` holds this hosted bundle's ProximityKit keychain funnel on `ProximityKeychainItem.SystemKeychain` (a row it stores is FernletFoundation's `KeychainItem`'s to read, under the class it wrote), and, since A1.1.10, the wall `noFernletFileNamesProximityKitsKeychainBackendDoor` holds that no file of `App`, of `FernletKit/Sources` but ProximityKit's own, or of `Tests` (each at a file floor) names the funnel's backend door, `installBackend(_:)`, as a call, a reference or a declaration, read over lexed code with its matcher fixtured; Its claims: a module allowlist of six names, with every one of the 28 FernletKit targets (and, since ProximityKit plan step A0.8.2, ProximityKit's test target, forbidden: no product links it) classified forbidden-or-permitted-with-a-reason and a walk of `Package.swift` that reds when a new module has no disposition (the two product names no target carries, the `FernletKit` umbrella and, since A1.0.1, the macOS check's `MacReady`, need none); an 86-spelling call zero-list (`ProximityPairSession` among them since A0.7.3) — the mesh/HealthKit/CloudKit/FoundationModels types, the ten radio verbs `ProximityRunSeamsTests` counts, the store-creation spellings, the `internal` `FernletStore` doors a second file in the app target can actually speak, and (added by item 3's verify) the 15-row clock-and-persistence family, which is where "schedule at handle + background, never on a timer" and "no new persisted surface" are actually enforced: the scheduling suite had carried those needles over three hand-named paths, which is a wall over three files rather than over the directory item 4's handler lands in; the identifier as a positive needle, exactly once, as code; a floor on the walk; and two drift cells — the ten copied radio verbs against the spellings that file still speaks, and all 37 app-declared needles against the declarations `App/Fernlet/` actually makes — so a rename cannot leave a needle nothing can contain. Item 4's verify fixes RETIRED one: `attachedHealthKitService` had zero production callers (four test assertions and nothing else), so a door that hands out the HealthKit gateway to any file able to name the store is gone from `FernletStore` and `HealthSyncCoordinator` together with its needle, and the three read-only doors the handler gained in its place — `hasCompleteScoringContext`, `publishedWidgetSnapshot()`, `publishedWidgetSnapshotIsForCurrentDay` — are on the PERMITTED list with the argument for each. The call list is the half that holds: the app target is one module, so `store.applyProximityRunPolicy(…)`, `store.meshNetworkManager`, `store.refreshWorkoutsFromHealth()` and `FernletStore.load(…)` all reach the prohibited thing with no import line at all, and an import-only wall is green over every one of them. ACCESS LEVEL is the second gate: the list's first shape named only the PRIVATE funnels (`runProximityPolicy`, `applyProximityRunPolicyFromView`, `healthSyncCoordinator`), which no second file can speak, and the verify caught it passing a probe that spoke the reachable ones — those three are kept as belt behind the internal braces. The line matcher keeps string INTERPOLATION bodies, because `"policy: \(store.reapplyProximityRunPolicy())"` runs the funnel exactly as the bare call does, and a table cell pins the stripper input-by-input. Reuses `S3BoundaryTests.importedModules` / `.containsAtIdentifierBoundary` so the two walls cannot disagree. | `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CompanionRefreshSchedulingTests.swift` | **P10 item 3**: the refresh scheduling seam driven end to end through two fakes, because nothing can make iOS deliver a `BGAppRefreshTask` to a unit test and plan §27.2 is explicit that the Simulator's answer to an app-refresh SUBMISSION is an open measurement rather than the continued-processing path's known `BGTaskSchedulerErrorDomain` 1. Claims the app's half: one registration with exactly the frozen identifier (a drift from `Info.plist` is an error nowhere — iOS just stops delivering); the request built to the 15-minute policy; the chain re-submitting on every handler run, read AT the completion so the order is pinned and not just the count; a refused registration and a refused submission each REPORTED, never swallowed, because an audit line is the only observable a dead chain ever produces; the pending-slot rule in both directions (three edges with no delivery ask ONCE, at the first edge's floor; the tail's request silences the next edge; a refused tail leaves nothing pending and the next edge asks again); the R2 EDGE bound, driven through refusals because with the slot rule that is the only shape an edge storm has, and its twin — the tail re-submitting through 65 deliveries with the edge budget spent; and **exactly-once completion as a five-row table** in P8's oracle shape, covering the expiry door and the system's own installed handler each once and twice, with the sixth order (an expiration landing while the task is still HELD, before the tail) as its own cell because it needs the re-entrant fake. Plus the boundary pins: the mesh's two protocols gained no member — their brace-matched bodies are FROZEN as normalised strings, because the first shape counted the word `func` and stayed green over a `var` requirement — `BGAppRefreshTask` has one home and `BGContinuedProcessingTask` is not it, the clock-and-persistence family still exists on `BackgroundRefreshBoundaryTests` and that wall's walk really reaches this seam's files, and the parsed `Info.plist` carries both the identifier and the `fetch` mode without having replaced the mesh's wildcard. The frozen identifier is asserted against the LITERAL `MBO.Fernlet.companion-refresh` rather than against `CompanionRefresh.taskIdentifier`, which put the constant on both sides of an `==` and could not fail. The `deliveryAbsorbed` arm is unreachable through the ordinary item-3 path (the handler completes synchronously) and is driven re-entrantly rather than shipped untested. What only a device can prove is named and claimed nowhere. | `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WidgetSnapshotContentEqualityTests.swift` | **P10 item 4**: the content/metadata split "reload timelines only on change" rests on. The trap it exists for: `WidgetSnapshot` is `Equatable` by synthesis and the synthesis includes `computedAt`, stamped `Date()` at every construction — so `==` is FALSE between two snapshots of an unchanged day a millisecond apart, and a handler that diffed with `==` would reload the widget every fifteen minutes forever with a green suite behind it. One cell proves the two notions are different in both directions at once; a flip table covers every content field and a separate cell pins that the table is WHOLE; a `Mirror` field-count pin reds when a field is added to the struct without being classified — the failure a hand-written comparison always has and never announces. The metadata claim is made MECHANICALLY rather than in prose: `computedAt` is counted as code under `App/FernletWidgets` (three sites, all writes, all in the shared-model twin), so a family that starts drawing it reds here instead of leaving the refresh silently skipping reloads a person can see. The mirror's own decision is counted through the `reloadTimelines` closure the type already injects, and a last cell pins that the unconditional `publish(_:)` path still reloads every time. Gated on the `s3-grep` CI step. |

Since ProximityKit plan step A1.1.10 its self-exclusion layer-1 cell sends the ghost the previous epoch's tag, which layers 2 and 3 would admit, beside a control sighting of the same tag under a name the engine never wore, which is admitted, so the ghost's drop is layer 1's alone. |
