and the host's platform facts (plan step A0.6), a second `ProximityHost` extension answering the doubles that state none, `proximityFallbackDisplayName` as `"Test device"` (A0.6.2), `makeProximityRangingProvider()` as a fresh `NoopRangingProvider()` (A0.6.3) and `proximitySupportDirectory` as the double's namespace's folder under Application Support, the folder the doubles always shared (A0.6.6). The rule its header states: a binding restores a call shape, never a value, with two stated exceptions, the identity door and the platform facts; a test that pins a value names `.fernlet` explicitly. A commit that takes a call shape out of ProximityKit adds its binding here, beside the API it restores; the shapes of the types that left ProximityKit for `FernletSocial` live in `FernletSocialTestBindings.swift`, and the identity's two initializers, which carry the app's escrow, in `FernletAppTestBindings.swift`, so this file imports neither `FernletSocial` nor the app. From ProximityKit plan step A1.1.4 to A1.3 the package's `TestSupport/PackageTestBindings.swift` was its replacement, each one of this file's declarations with `.packageTest` for `.fernlet`, held declaration by declaration by the selector wall (T6); the package's file is its repository's since, and this one is Fernlet's own again. The re-pin to 0.1.1 (2026-10-17) retired three shapes whose only callers were suites the package holds at that tag: `MeshLedgerAdoption.bootstrapVerifier`/`adopt`, the chunk door's initializer and `MeshRoutedContentHasher()`. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletSocialTestBindings.swift` | The test target's home for the old call shapes of the types that left ProximityKit for `FernletSocial` (ProximityKit plan steps A0.4 and A0.5), and of the shapes built from `FernletSocial`'s declarations, under the core bindings' rule (a binding restores a call shape, never a value, passing `.fernlet`): `PresenceManager.firstName(of:)` over `firstName(of:in: .fernlet)`; `SessionMessageStore.receiveIncoming(id:senderFingerprint:senderDisplayName:text:sentAt:seenAt:)` over `.fernlet`'s peer-name policy; `MeshRoutedTypeRegistry.increment1` and `MeshRoutedAckStageTable.increment1` (as values and over a namespace's routed types), built from `FernletRoutedTypes`' rows; the chat's `allowIncomingRoutedText(_:from:)`; the host doubles' `meshRoutedTypeEntries`, Fernlet's rows over their namespace; and the recipe-share manager's seam initializer, `init(store:makeSession:identity:)`, over a `ProximityPairSession` under the recipe profile on the host's own namespace (the rule's one stated exception, as `init(store:)` builds it), with one forwarder per test seam of the session's, so the recipe suites keep their spelling; and the presence manager's seams that reached what moved into ProximityKit's presence engine at plan step A1.1 (`makeSession`, `postureMint`, `presencePosture`, `discoveryInfoForTesting()`, `registerOwnEphemeralPeerNameForTesting(_:)`, `handleDiscoveredPeerForTesting(_:)`, and the two heart seams over the engine's channel), each a forwarder to the engine's own. `@testable import FernletSocial`, so `ProximityNamespaceTestBindings.swift` keeps ProximityKit's shapes alone. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletMeshTestSupport.swift` | **ProximityKit plan step A0.5.5**: `FernletMeshRig`, the one way the test target builds Fernlet's mesh as the app does — `FernletSocial`'s `FernletMeshFeatures` over a mesh the manager's internal designated initializer builds with the radio and identity a rig injects, and the pending photo corpus's key — checking every installation the composition owes its mesh. Its rule: a rig node keeps the composition, never a bare mesh, because the mesh holds its session observer weakly and would otherwise run every moment with no feature leg; a suite that wants a bare engine builds `MeshNetworkManager(store:)` explicitly. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletAppTestBindings.swift` | The test target's home for the call shapes that need the app's own types (ProximityKit plan step A0.4), under the core bindings' rule (a binding restores a call shape, never a value): `IdentityService.init()` and `init(keychainService:)` as convenience initializers over `init(namespace:keychainService:provisioningParticipant:)` with `.fernlet` and a fresh `SealedBackupEscrowKey`, the custody every identity the app builds carries, so the suites' constructions compile unchanged and keep the escrow behaviour they were written against. `@testable import Fernlet`, so `ProximityNamespaceTestBindings.swift` never imports the app. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CryptographicDomainSeparationTests.swift` | The domain CONTRACT, as a property of the whole 47-entry registry: every test is a negative that requires a cross-domain operation to FAIL. Spelling uniqueness, all-pairs key/tag/digest separation, AAD separation with the key held constant, the production v3 seal's own AAD binding, transcript discrimination, and prefix-freedom (`signingBytes` matches by `starts(with:)`, and several call sites build AAD by bare concatenation). The inventory is checked against `CryptographicPurpose.swift` on disk, not trusted. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/HealthKitLifecycleBoundaryTests.swift` | The two `269003c` lifecycle invariants no behavioural test can reach: the observed-change refresh still coalesces (pending set + newest-wins `cancel()` + the named `healthChangeCoalescingWindow`), and every `HealthKitService` construction in the tree is inventoried with its role — one long-lived, three transient per-write, three injection fallbacks, one `#Preview`. A source scan, and says so. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/PasteboardBoundaryTests.swift` | Pasteboard grep-wall: every shipping write to `UIPasteboard.general` must go through `setItems(_:options:)` with `.localOnly: true`. A plain `UIPasteboard.general.string = …` is Handoff-synced to every device on the account, and there is no API to read an item's options back — this static check is the whole enforcement. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/TestHookBoundaryTests.swift` | Debug/test-hook grep-wall: every shipping line reading a launch hook of one of six families, Fernlet's `FERNLET_UI_TEST_`, `FERNLET_SKIP_`, `FERNLET_MESH` and `FERNLET_PROBE` and ProximityKit's `PROXIMITYKIT_UI_TEST_` and `PROXIMITYKIT_MESH` (its six keys, since plan step A1.0), must sit inside an open `#if DEBUG`, with a global floor and a floor per family, and `UITestSupport`'s `#else` block must define a no-op for every `static var` its DEBUG half declares; rides no CI line. **Since ProximityKit plan step A1.1.15:** the scan reads every shipping root but ProximityKit's folder (`ProximityKitSources.folder`; 552 files, 64 reads), whose own reads ProximityKit's package suite `ProximitySourceWallTests` holds; ProximityKit's two families here are what Fernlet's code reads of its keys, `PROXIMITYKIT_MESH` at the app banner's 2 (5 before) and `PROXIMITYKIT_UI_TEST_` at 0 (3 before), kept so that TH1 still refuses an app-side read of one outside DEBUG; the planted-token cell also holds the exclusion to its samples. **2026-10-15 (ProximityKit plan step A1.3):** the scan reads every shipping root with no exclusion (ProximityKit is a package dependency), and the exclusion's samples left with `ProximityKitSources`. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/PhotoDirectoryIsolationTests.swift` | Grep-wall keeping every test `FernletStore` on its own own-photo root. The three own-photo corpora are shared on-disk state that `resetAll`/`deleteAllData`/the duress wipe all clear, and parallel suites share one process — a store on the process-wide root has its photos deleted out from under it by any concurrent wipe. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/PrivacyPolicyParityTests.swift` | Pins the privacy-policy triple in sync (in-app view, `Docs/Privacy-Policy.md`, `Site/privacy/index.html`): same effective date, same load-bearing clauses. Exists because the in-app copy once shipped without the verifiability paragraph the other two had. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FernletLockScopeTests.swift` | Per-surface unlock scopes: one unlock covers ONE locked surface. Before scoping, unlocking the progress-photo strip also opened the Private tab, with only an `onDisappear` re-lock in between — which a covering sheet or scene transition legitimately suppresses. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressLockTests.swift` | Duress PIN steps 1–4: the duress verifier's own salt, the unconditional derivation that closes the timing oracle, duress-FIRST ordering in every credential entry point, and the keyless decoy session. The property under test is indistinguishability from a benign unlock — same state, same audit line, no attempt/cooldown residue — while never handing over the real content key. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressDecoyAndWipeTests.swift` | Duress steps 5–6: the decoy must change NOTHING on disk (an in-memory flag riding the existing hide machinery, so the real passcode restores every surface exactly), and the silent wipe's crypto-erase must actually destroy. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressRecoveryTests.swift` | Duress steps 7–8: `DuressMode.recoveryLock` destroys every local way into the content key while KEEPING the sealed corpus and a recovery blob only the user's own second device can open, plus the two in-person ceremonies that enroll that device and get the key back. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressHardeningTests.swift` | The properties the duress PIN was supposed to have and did not — each suite pins one confirmed defect from the adversarial review (e.g. a duress session can never hold the `.appLockSettings` scope, the one screen that announces a duress code exists). |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/DuressSetupUITests.swift` | The duress setup screen's testable half: `DuressSetupAvailability` (which response may be armed, whether the recovery device may be un-enrolled) and the rejection copy the view surfaces rather than restates. Unit-level despite the name. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/BackupExclusionLaunchGateTests.swift` | The default-on backup-exclusion launch gate — fresh installs default excluded, existing installs get a one-time honest prompt, nobody is silently flipped — plus the `LocalFernletRepository` day-blob exclusion that closes the last local Fernlet-data file the Privacy & Data toggle did not reach. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/OwnPhotoKeyBindingTests.swift` | The follow-on flip: the own-photos at-rest key becomes device-bound and the dual-open fallback goes away. Runs against REAL keychain rows (isolated `UserDefaults` for the latch/consent) because the thing under test is a keychain attribute. Since the crypto standardization round retired `OwnPhotoKeyMigrator`, the gate's first half reads `MediaAtRestFormatMigrationLatch`, and `aWallKeySealedOwnFileNoLongerBlocksTheGateAndIsLostAcrossTheFlip` pins what that swap COST — a recorded trade, not a surprise. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SealedPhotoBackupTests.swift` | The own-photo escrow route: one sealed CloudKit record per photo id plus a sealed manifest written LAST as the commit marker, driven over a mock record database with a planted escrow identity. Pins incrementality — sealing photo B leaves A's record byte-identical, which a chunked scheme cannot do. Also the store's legs (bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §1, §2): Retry re-runs a failed enable, Delete Everything reaches a corpus a failed enable committed and a backup the presence probe finds, and a new phone restores around its own photos. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SealedBackupPayloadCoverageTests.swift` | Journal narratives and intimacy logs as first-class sealed-backup payloads, driven through `SealedBackupCoordinator` with a fake context: insert-into-empty, the one-way divergence latch, the locked-key deferral, and the empty-store-clobber guard, all against isolated sealed stores. Also the app-lock-reset owner hold over restores AND re-uploads (the escrow adopt included), and the paused period export (review C-U2-R1, C-U2-R2). Since unit B2 the intimacy cases are the v2 merge: a populated store is merged into (`intimacyRestoreMergesIntoAPopulatedStore`), a hidden store is refused at the seam and never reads as empty, the journal's day-skeleton writeback no longer gates the intimacy merge, and an intimacy backup that was off at an app-lock reset uploads through the engine and is re-sealed at the next visit after an escrow adopt. Since unit B3 the journal cases are the v2 engine's too: E1 holds an empty store's export until its restore resolved (then empty is real), entries no key opens pause before any save, the merge runs on a device already in use and into a populated store, every restore and export is held after an app-lock reset until the owner's release (which merges the copy into the post-reset store before the union backs up), a diverged store seeds its marker resolved, and duress refuses the journal merge. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/IntimacyHealthWriteTests.swift` | `IntimacyHealthWrite`: both halves landing leave no caveat; a failed sample says Apple Health was not updated and never links; a landed sample whose link mark fails is its own outcome, never a Health failure (anomaly re-triage 2026-10-17). |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/IntimacyLogRepositoryTests.swift` | The sealed-backup surface on `IntimacyLogRepository`: keyless row count, paged reader in a TOTAL order, the one-way "this device has diverged" latch (now the one-time seed of the intimate-log restore marker), and — unit B2, BV12 — the keyless id snapshot, the classified read by id, the restore MERGE (absent inserted with its stamps and a capped note, local kept with only a missing link filled, dead replaced, never a delete, idempotent, all-or-nothing) and the keyless delete by id. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/CoachPlanExchangeTests.swift` | The manual coach exchange (clipboard export → pasted `CoachPlan` → dated `PlannedWorkout` rows). The paste path takes untrusted text, so most of the suite is what the importer REFUSES: oversize blobs, truncated JSON, plans over the day cap, exercises with no safety metadata. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MessagesCatalogPublisherTests.swift` | The app's Messages catalog publisher over a library it cannot share whole (2026-09-23): a recipe or plan the exchange rules refuse (a web import with more than 24 servings, a plan name past the card bound) is left out while every shareable item still publishes — one refusal used to throw out of the whole publish and leave the extension on a stale catalog. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/ExchangeMessageEnvelopeV2Tests.swift` | The version-2 Messages envelope: the wire checked against Foundation's own `NSData` deflater/inflater, the tight 3,719-byte frame bound, inflate bombs / truncation / lying lengths / foreign frames, invalid documents, capacity on a realistic 40-step recipe (v1: 5 steps, v2: all 40), and two version-1 cards minted by the untouched 2026-09-23 build plus the version-1 content-hash pre-images as literals — the old wire can never silently stop opening. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/ExchangeMultipartRecipeTests.swift` | A recipe made in parts as an exchange packet and a Messages card: one-part recipes stay packet v1 byte-for-byte, recipes in parts are v2, the v2 content hash checked against an independently built pre-image, each version limited to its own shape, and the salad-with-homemade-dressing fixture fitting a card with room to spare. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MessagesReceivedItemTests.swift` | `FernletMessagesReceivedItem.resolve` (2026-09-30, the owner's "receiving a recipe pops up blank" report): a card minted by the sender's own encoder opens as that recipe or plan with the packet's own title (multipart parts and the suggested start day kept), the golden version-1 and version-2 cards still open, and a missing, foreign, future-version, truncated, re-encoded, oversize or hash-failing URL is `.invalid` — never an empty screen, never a throw. Gated in CI on the `messages-envelope` line of `s3-wall.yml`. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/LocaleCorrectnessTests.swift` | Locale Phase 0: the month grid must start its week where `Calendar.firstWeekday` says. `veryShortWeekdaySymbols` is always Sunday-indexed, so rendering it unrotated over `firstWeekday - 1` blanks put every day cell under the wrong letter across most of Europe. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/LocaleTolerantNumberTests.swift` | The shared free-text number parser behind every decimal field: iOS shows `.decimalPad` with the locale's separator, so es/fr/de users type `"2,5"` and bare `Double("2,5")` is nil — the value was silently dropped. Pins both spellings, grouped forms, the ambiguous `"1,500"` split, and the charset guard that keeps hex/inf/nan spellings out of clinical samples. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/MicronutrientBoundsTests.swift` | Micronutrients arrive from two untrusted sources (a peer's recipe payload, a web page's JSON-LD) and were carried into the synced snapshot unbounded. Covers the wire half (`sanitizedForImport()` drops an implausible amount to "not measured" rather than fabricating a clamp) and the presentation half, which must be TOTAL because `Int(_: Double)` traps and already-poisoned days are synced. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WebScrapingKitTests.swift` | `WebScrapingKit`'s HTML/JSON-LD extraction primitives — net-new in the 2026-08-18 security round. The old `scriptContents(from:)` regex retried from every `<script` position, so a page with a few hundred thousand unclosed tags cost ~N²/2 comparisons for zero matches and could stall the main actor into a watchdog kill. Flood cases prove linearity; equivalence cases pin the rewrite's output against the regex it replaced. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/JournalScoringParityTests.swift` | Journaling scores the same whatever kind of day it was (2026-09-23): every `FeelingTag` earns the same journal component, above no entry, never below its retired value; the overall score no longer depends on the tag; the tag survives as the unweighted `mood` reading the period bridge reads (with the legacy-row fallback). Check-ins count half (2026-09-24): a day of one-tap mood check-ins only earns 0.775 (half the credit above no entry), a written entry that day the full 1.0; cells for check-in only / check-in + entry / entry only / none, the unchanged mood reading, the sealed and pre-marker entries that stay full credit, and both store score paths. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WeightManagementDeficitTests.swift` | Weight Management's deficit: 10% by default and user-adjustable 0–20% in 5% steps (owner, 2026-09-24): the research note's worked examples, each choice's target, the 1,200/1,500 kcal + RMR floor at every choice (the RMR half binding at 20%), "no deficit rather than a surplus" under the floor, a 1,000-profile invariant grid per choice, range/step enforcement, the settings round-trip, no under-18 exception (owner), and the goal card's copy for every choice. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/WeightManagementDeficitSettingTests.swift` | The user-adjustable deficit from the app's side (2026-09-24): the Nutrition targets row (`WeightManagementDeficitControl`) shows only for Weight Management, reads the value in effect, and goes out of effect under a pinned calorie target; the store setter saves durably and enforces the range; the goal card and the targets card read one number (with a source pin on Settings' wiring); Settings search finds the row. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SickReadinessTests.swift` | Marking today unwell forces readiness to `needs rest` (spec §6a), at once and across the save rebuild; the recommendation is `.light`, never nil; Home's and the launch thought's rest line, the companion prompt's rest-first ordering, the Trends display fork; Move's light-only chips, never-push copy (`MoveRestDayCopy`) and the un-guided rest plan. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SidecarBackupExclusionTests.swift` | Bug confirmation 2026-10-15 §5: the closeness, friend-state, moderation and activity ledgers are excluded from iCloud device backups through each store's own write, and an earlier build's file is healed when its store opens. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/SillyStatsTests.swift` | Silly stats: the cookie table and food matching, the tally fold (tonnage, activities, sleep, care, days known), reveal thresholds and ladders, inflected copy, the companion pacer's cadence, the frozen kind tokens and the one-time Home widget append. |

### Test Mocks

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/Mocks/MockMultipeerTransport.swift` | Single-peer scripted `PeerTransport` for coordinator handshake tests. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/Mocks/FakePeerTransport.swift` | Deterministic multi-endpoint transport fabric: `VirtualClock`, `FakePeerNetwork` (links, latency, partition/heal), `FakePeerTransport`. No wall-clock sleeps — the foundation of the P4 partition suite. From ProximityKit plan step A0.8.2 to A1.3 a twin of the package tests' copy, held equal but for imports by the selector wall; Fernlet's own file since, which 22 FernletTests files use and `FakePeerTransportTests` (FernletTests' copy of the package's suite, on `mesh-batteries`) checks. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/FakePeerTransportTests.swift` | ProximityKit plan step A1.3, on the `mesh-batteries` CI line: `FakePeerTransportTests` (10 cells), FernletTests' copy of the package suite of that name (ProximityKit's `PeerTransportNeutralityTests.swift`), over Fernlet's own `Mocks/FakePeerTransport.swift`, the fabric Fernlet's acceptance batteries and partition scenarios build on: latency, partition without telling the sender, heal without replay, an in-flight cut, a 2/2 split, latency ordering, the paused-invite refusal, disconnect against partition, and the clock as the only source of time. Kept for as long as the batteries ride the fabric: a fake nobody checks is a source of false confidence. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletTests/Mocks/MockRangingProvider.swift` | Controllable `RangingProvider` mock used in coordinator and session tests; without hardware it throws the protocol-level `RangingError.hardwareUnsupported` (since ProximityKit plan step A0.6.3; it named the iOS-only `NIRangingSession.RangingError` before). From ProximityKit plan step A0.8.3 to A1.3 a twin of the package tests' copy (which `NearbyRangingSessionTests` uses, in ProximityKit's repository since), held equal but for imports by the selector wall; Fernlet's own file since, which 29 FernletTests files use. |

### ProximityKit Package Tests

ProximityKit's own package tests (its `ProximityKitTests` target, with its test support and its suite inventory, `Tests/ProximityKitTests.tsv`) lived under `FernletKit/Tests/` from ProximityKit plan step A0.8 and left with the package at plan step A1.3: they are [bowman-mw/proximitykit](https://github.com/bowman-mw/proximitykit)'s, run by its own CI on every lane, at the tag Fernlet pins (`0.1.1`). Fernlet keeps one copy of a package suite, `FakePeerTransportTests` (Unit Tests above), over its own copy of the fake fabric its acceptance batteries build on.

The three bar tests were red on main on iPhone 17, iOS 26.5 (the bar rode the keyboard everywhere); the toast test is red with the bar pinned but the toast left inside the bar's inset (Undo at y 731.7, behind the keyboard). Frames and screenshots attached. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletUITests/KeyboardDoneAccessoryUITests.swift` | Every text field inside a `NavigationStack` gets exactly one keyboard "Done" accessory (2026-10-01): `keyboardDoneToolbar()` reaches only fields in its own hosting controller, so the one declared outside the Food tab's stack, and `fernletSheetChrome`'s around a sheet that wraps a stack, reached no page. Food → Recipe book search, the pushed manual editor's name and Qty pad, the recipe and recipe-book sheets, Settings → Goal & nutrition's calorie pad, First aid's worry editor, and the stack-less Sleep sheet as the control: one button labelled "Done" just above the keys, and tapping it puts the keyboard away. A second pass (2026-10-01) covers hosts no sheet chrome reaches, which had no Done at all: the day edit sheet's sleep-hours decimal pad and the pre-log meal review's Qty pad (sheets presented bare by a page), onboarding's age number pad (the window's root view), and the Private tab's Worry box composer (a tab page with no stack). |

| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletUITests/WorkoutLocationUITests.swift` | UI test for gym-location delete/rename, asserting a deletion persists after closing and reopening the sheet. |
| `Fernlet/Tests/FernletUITests/ItemCreationFlowUITests.swift` | UI test for the split item-creation flow: the drawing-only editor leading to a separate naming/shop-listing confirmation step. |

The copy button is only checked for existence and hittability — tapping it presents a data-leaves-the-device confirmation, and a UI test should not be what teaches anyone that dialog is skippable. |

layer and live in-source at `Documentation.docc/`; the tables below are the planning-doc layer.

### Orientation

| File | Purpose |

| `Docs/FernletSpecificationV3.md` | Canonical product & architecture specification, reconciled with the current implementation on 2026-09-14. Explicit current-status blocks override retained historical prototype notes. |
| `Docs/ImplementationPlan.md` | Historical phase definitions and rationale, plus the reconciled 2026-09-14 status table and current release/mesh ordering. |

### Privacy commitments, walls, and process

| File | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Docs/Privacy-Policy.md` | The published privacy policy text, mirrored by the in-app view and the hosted page; the triple is kept in sync by `PrivacyPolicyParityTests`. |
| `Docs/Verifiability.md` | Standing commitment: the bridge between the promises in the privacy policy and the machinery that backs them. Every guarantee names the exact command, test, or file an outside auditor can run, so "trust us" can be replaced with "run this". The repo is public and `fernlet.com` is deployed. |
| `Docs/Release-Process.md` | Standing process, and the governance half of `Verifiability.md`: branch protection, code-owner review, signed release tags, checksum publication, and the two one-time publish steps. The walls make a privacy regression loud; this makes shipping one attributable and gated. |
| `Docs/No-Tracking-Wall.md` | The enforced no-tracking boundary: permitted network destinations and why each exists, mechanical vs. policy enforcement, and how to add a destination. Backed by `NoTrackingBoundaryTests`. |
| `Docs/PrivacyWipeCoverage.md` | The enforced delete-all coverage checklist, backed by `PrivacyWipeCoverageTests`. |
| `Docs/Crypto-Domain-Separation.md` | The cryptographic domain registry: the inventory of its 81 entries across five families (the tables list 69 and name the twelve they lack), the rule that purpose spellings are FROZEN protocol data (never localized, never assembled from input, never casually renamed), transcript framing and the `91c3956` mismatch it records, which formats are version-marked and the fact that NOTHING reads an older one any more, the procedure and review checklist for adding a domain, and — at length — what the two boundary suites can and cannot catch, including the 6 remaining `// cryptographic-domain:` escape-hatch sites in Fernlet's code — all annotations, no reader left — and how the count reached them (ProximityKit's one, the QUIC transport's X.509 self-signature, is counted by its own package suite `ProximitySourceWallTests` since ProximityKit plan step A1.1.16, and both walls read Fernlet's shipping roots, which hold no ProximityKit source since plan step A1.3). Backed by `CryptographicPurposeBoundaryTests` (a purpose is USED) and `CryptographicDomainSeparationTests` (the registry's values are USABLE for separation). §8 (2026-10-02) covers ProximityKit's namespace: labels the host supplies as source literals, roles ProximityKit fixes, soundness judged when a namespace is built and acted on at run time (under an unsound one no identity provisions or wraps a group key and no radio starts), `.fernlet` in FernletConnections, a host's feature salts (minted by `featureKeyDerivationSalt(_:)`, declared in the family's feature group, judged with the protocol's labels, and the only salts `IdentityService.pairSecret(with:purpose:)` derives under; `.fernlet` declares the heart and presence pair salts), a host's own signature labels (minted by `featureSignature(_:framing:)`, length-prefixed or raw prefix only, declared nowhere; Fernlet's six twin registry entries, and ProximityKit's identity signs under nothing else since plan step A1.0), the 40 registry twins (38 protocol labels and the two feature salts) `ProximityNamespaceGoldenTests` holds equal until they retire, and the epoch label `fernlet.mesh.epoch.v1`, now inside the joint prefix check. |
| `Docs/Power-of-10-Swift.md` | The Power-of-10 wall: NASA's ten rules adapted to Swift/SwiftUI, rule by rule — what is enforced by `Scripts/power-of-10-scan.py` + `PowerOfTenBoundaryTests` (zero baseline + reasoned allowlist) and what is enforced by review. Read before writing any function. |
| `Docs/SPM-Module-Carveup-Plan.md` | The `FernletKit` carve-up: the S3 compile-time privacy wall and cross-platform layering. One optional item remains (§14 AI-file inversions). |
| `Docs/App-Privacy-Nutrition-Labels.md` | Draft answers for the App Store Connect App Privacy questionnaire (owner-entered). |
| `Docs/Export-Compliance-Encryption.md` | Encryption export compliance: mass-market, ECCN 5D992.c, self-classified under License Exception ENC with no CCATS. Materially revised 2026-08-19 — the determination held, its *filing consequences* did not: §740.17(e)(3) was rewritten in 2021 to cover only encryption components and "executable software", so the annual February 1 report is almost certainly not owed, and the earlier text of this document asserted a recurring legal obligation that does not exist. |
| `Docs/Export-Self-Classification-Memo.md` | The self-classification memorandum itself — the record §740.17(b)(1) obliges the developer to have made and be able to produce. Five-year retention past the last export. Engineering and regulatory analysis, explicitly not legal advice; §8 lists the two questions that warrant counsel. |

## Scripts And CI

| Path | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/spm-wall-check.sh` | The iOS strict build, one of two (`Scripts/macos-build-check.sh`, the Mac-ready set on native macOS, is the second): the app's `xcodebuild build-for-testing` with `DIAGNOSE_MISSING_TARGET_DEPENDENCIES=YES_ERROR` (S3 wall — a forbidden cross-wall `import` fails the build) plus `SUPPRESS_WARNINGS=NO SWIFT_TREAT_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS=YES GCC_TREAT_WARNINGS_AS_ERRORS=YES` (Power-of-10 rule 10 — every warning, including the package warnings Xcode otherwise hides, is an error). `PowerOfTenBoundaryTests.r10WallCheckScriptBuildsStrict` reads its one `xcodebuild build-for-testing` command, never its `echo` of the flags. Run pre-merge and in CI. **2026-10-11 (ProximityKit plan step A0.8.1):** builds `-workspace Fernlet.xcworkspace -scheme Fernlet`, not `-project App/Fernlet.xcodeproj`: only the workspace lets the scheme see a package test target, and `Scripts/run-gated-suites.sh` runs through the same workspace, so with the default derived data the two read one build (the container is pinned by `CIGateSelectorBoundaryTests`; a run with `FERNLET_DERIVED_DATA` reads the build at that path, which this script never takes). **2026-10-11 (A0.8.2):** the scheme's `ProximityKitTests` testable is compiled here, strict, beside `FernletTests` and `FernletUITests`. **2026-10-15 (ProximityKit plan step A1.3):** the scheme's `ProximityKitTests` testable left with the package; the build compiles ProximityKit's tag (`0.1.0`, resolved from `FernletKit/Package.resolved`) with warnings as errors, and the workspace stays for developing it beside Fernlet (plan step A1.5). |
| `Fernlet/Fernlet.xcworkspace` | ProximityKit plan step A0.8.1's root workspace, in CODEOWNERS: `FernletKit` and `App/Fernlet.xcodeproj` side by side. Kept at plan step A1.3 for developing ProximityKit beside Fernlet (A1.5): a local checkout of the `proximitykit` package dragged into it overrides the pinned tag on one machine, never committed, while CI always builds the tag. The strict build (`Scripts/spm-wall-check.sh`), every CI test run (`Scripts/run-gated-suites.sh`) and the documented build and test commands go through it, since a build and a run through different containers land in different derived data; its packages resolve from `FernletKit/Package.resolved` (it holds no resolved file of its own: one there would be a mirror Xcode rewrites), and `CIGateSelectorBoundaryTests` holds every pin that file shares with the app project's (`cryptoswift` and `proximitykit` at least), which `-project` and `xcodebuild archive` (`Scripts/release-checksum.sh`) read, to one revision. `Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh` and `Scripts/release-checksum.sh` keep `-project` on purpose: neither runs a test. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/power-of-10-scan.py` | Power-of-10 mechanical scan (rules 2/4/5/6/7/8/9 + assertion-density floor + allowlist hygiene); zero-violation baseline; `--advisory` lists the review-only signals; `--json` for tooling. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/power-of-10-allowlist.json` | The single reasoned allowlist read by both the scanner and `PowerOfTenBoundaryTests`; every entry names the invariant that makes the exemption safe. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/doc-coverage-scan.py` | Scans Swift sources for type declarations with no adjacent `///` doc comment. Zero undocumented is the enforced baseline; runs in the Power-of-10 CI workflow. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/render-imessage-icon.py` | Renders the Messages extension's `iMessage App Icon` set (all twelve slots, exact pixel sizes, no alpha) from the Home Screen icon: lifts the sprig off its measured cream background, turns it onto the 4:3 diagonal, and scales it to the largest size that keeps 6% clearance inside the Messages "+" menu's superellipse mask (measured on the iOS 26.5 simulator). The set's source of truth — re-run it if the Home Screen icon changes; deterministic. The only script that needs Pillow + NumPy. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/sync-string-catalogs.sh` | Repopulates the `.xcstrings` catalogs outside Xcode via `xcstringstool sync` over the `.stringsdata` a build emits. Run after changing user-facing strings and commit the catalog diff with the code; `--check` for CI. Additive — vanished keys are marked stale, never deleted. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/run-gated-suites.sh` | Every CI test step's runner (P5 review finding 4): runs the named suites against the already-built bundle with `-resultBundlePath`, then reads the bundle's own `totalTestCount` and refuses a green run under the step's floor — because `-only-testing:` matches suite names exactly, matches nothing on a misspelling or a deleted suite, and then prints `TEST EXECUTE SUCCEEDED` over zero tests. `CIGateSelectorBoundaryTests` is the static half. Usable locally: `Scripts/run-gated-suites.sh <label> <min> <Selector>…`. **2026-10-11 (ProximityKit plan step A0.8.1):** runs through `-workspace Fernlet.xcworkspace`, the container `Scripts/spm-wall-check.sh` builds; a selector is a `FernletTests` suite's bare name or `ProximityKitTests/<Suite>` for ProximityKit's own package test target (every other shape, and a suite named twice on one line, exits 2 before `xcodebuild` runs); one run may name both, and the floor reads their summed `totalTestCount`; and the suite census writes the bundle's tests tree to a file beside it, counts test cases per `<bundle>/<Type>` off each top-level Test Suite's `nodeIdentifierURL`, prints the per-bundle split on its `==>` line, and refuses a named suite that ran no test case (a mixed line's total can hide one) and a tree whose suites it cannot read. **2026-10-11 (A0.8.5):** the census reads a top-level suite only off a URL of exactly the measured shape whose bundle is the enclosing one, and names any other, or a test case outside any suite, as a tree it cannot read (every tree the A0.8.4 gate measured reads with none); `--check-log` refuses a log that is not there; the selector's identifier class is spelled out, so a non-ASCII selector is refused in any locale, as the selector wall refuses it; and `FERNLET_DERIVED_DATA` is documented as the derived data of a build made with the same path, which `Scripts/spm-wall-check.sh` never takes. **2026-10-15 (ProximityKit plan step A1.3):** one selector shape again, a bare FernletTests suite, emitted as `-only-testing:FernletTests/<Suite>`; `ProximityKitTests/<Suite>` is refused before xcodebuild runs. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/run-gated-suites-selftest.sh` | Negative test proving the restart guard in `run-gated-suites.sh` is load-bearing on its LIVE branch, not only at its `--check-log` seam (P10 item 5): stubs `xcodebuild` and `xcrun` on `PATH` so the gate reads a green-looking run whose count clears the floor, and asserts the script still exits non-zero when the run output carries `Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout` — a restart re-runs suites, so the bundle's `totalTestCount` is inflated and every floor above it stops proving anything. Four assertions: live negative/positive and seam negative/positive. Needs no Simulator, no build and no Xcode; runs on the enforcement self-test step of `s3-wall.yml`. **2026-10-11 (ProximityKit plan step A0.8.1):** eight assertions — the stub `xcrun` also answers the tests tree (one with a `FernletTests` and a `ProximityKitTests` suite, one with the first only) and the stub `xcodebuild` logs its arguments, truncated before every run: besides the four, a two-target run passes through `-workspace Fernlet.xcworkspace` (never `-project`) with each selector in its own target, the same selectors with the package suite absent from the tree are refused by the census, four other selector shapes and a duplicate each exit 2 with `xcodebuild` never run. **2026-10-11 (A0.8.5):** still eight assertions: the `FernletTests` suite's display name is not its type name; assertion 4 also refuses one `FernletTests` suite that ran nothing beside one its bundle ran, naming it alone, and a tree in an unmeasured shape, named as unreadable rather than as a suite that ran nothing; 5 refuses a non-ASCII selector, every gate running under the UTF-8 locale; 7 refuses a missing log at the seam. Shown red against seven runner mutants: a census per bundle, one over package selectors only, suites keyed by display name, the old URL reading, the ranged class, the old seam, and every selector emitted as a `FernletTests` one. **2026-10-15 (ProximityKit plan step A1.3):** the `both.json` tree (a FernletTests and a ProximityKitTests suite) gives way to `two.json` (two FernletTests suites): assertion 3 runs two bare suites, 4 names the one suite of two that ran nothing and the unreadable tree, and 5 refuses `ProximityKitTests/<Suite>` with the other shapes. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/release-checksum.sh` | Builds a release archive, emits SHA-256 checksums, and cuts a signed tag ([Release-Process.md](Release-Process.md), [Verifiability.md](Verifiability.md) §2). Honest limit, stated in the script itself: byte-exact verification of an App Store binary is impossible — Apple re-signs and re-encrypts every submission. What this gives is the self-build baseline: the signed tag pins the source, the checksums let two independent builders compare for drift, and a user can sideload their own build of the tag. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/spm-wall-selftest.sh` | Negative test proving the wall is load-bearing: plants a forbidden `import PrivateHealthStore` in `AIProviders`, asserts the build fails, then reverts. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/macos-build-check.sh` | The second strict build (ProximityKit plan step A0.6.5): `xcodebuild build -scheme MacReady -destination 'generic/platform=macOS'` from `FernletKit/`, the Mac-ready set the `MacReady` product names (FernletFoundation, FernletCrypto and FernletDomainModel, each by name; ProximityKit was a fourth until plan step A1.3, and its own repository's CI builds it on macOS since) for native macOS 26, arm64 and x86_64, with `DIAGNOSE_MISSING_TARGET_DEPENDENCIES=YES_ERROR` and the three rule-10 overrides, into its own derived data (`FernletKit/build/macos-check`, gitignored, or `FERNLET_MACOS_DERIVED_DATA`; never `FERNLET_DERIVED_DATA`); after a passing build it requires native macOS products and no Mac Catalyst ones, since `generic/platform=macOS` also matches Catalyst. `xcodebuild`, not `swift build`, because the SwiftPM command line leaves the set's string catalogs (FernletFoundation's and FernletDomainModel's) unprocessed. `ProximityPlatformBoundaryTests` W6 and `PowerOfTenBoundaryTests.r10MacOSBuildCheckScriptBuildsStrict` read its one `xcodebuild build` command. Run by the pre-push hook and, through its self-test, in CI. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/macos-build-selftest.sh` | Negative test proving the macOS check is a native macOS build of the Mac-ready set (A0.6.5): plants `import UIKit` in a throwaway FernletFoundation file (ProximityKit's until plan step A1.3), FernletFoundation being one of the three modules the check's `MacReady` product names, asserts the check fails with xcodebuild's `unable to resolve module dependency: 'UIKit'` (an iOS or Mac Catalyst build, or a build of nothing, would pass), removes the probe (trap) and asserts the clean tree passes. Runs on `s3-wall.yml`'s "The Mac-ready set on native macOS" step. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/install-git-hooks.sh` | Once per clone: points `core.hooksPath` at the versioned `Scripts/git-hooks` so pushes run the wall check and the macOS build check; its texts name both legs' one-push bypasses, `SKIP_S3_WALL_CHECK=1` and `SKIP_MACOS_BUILD_CHECK=1`. |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/git-hooks/pre-push` | Versioned pre-push hook, three legs that never end one another early (the pushed files are read from stdin once, before either build leg decides): `power-of-10-scan.py` on every push (`SKIP_P10_SCAN=1`); `spm-wall-check.sh` when a push touches any Swift/manifest/project file (`SKIP_S3_WALL_CHECK=1`); and, since ProximityKit plan step A0.6.5, `macos-build-check.sh` when it touches the manifest or its resolved file, the Mac-ready set (FernletCrypto, FernletDomainModel, FernletFoundation) or either macOS script (`SKIP_MACOS_BUILD_CHECK=1`), a leg whose invocation, default and relevance pattern `ProximityPlatformBoundaryTests` W6 pins (the latter two since A0.6.8). Since ProximityKit plan step A0.8.1 the wall leg's pattern also matches `Fernlet.xcworkspace/` and the shared schemes, which decide what the strict build compiles, and since A1.3 both `Package.resolved` files, whose pinned ProximityKit revision it compiles (the package's `FernletKit/Tests/` left the pattern with the package's tests). |
| `Fernlet/Scripts/branded-catalog/` | Python pipeline converting the USDA branded dataset into the curated and On-Demand-Resource SQLite catalogs. |

| `Fernlet/.github/workflows/power-of-10.yml` | CI workflow running the Power-of-10 mechanical scan, the doc-coverage scan and — since 2026-09-24 — the accessibility scanner (`Scripts/accessibility-scan.py`, the canonical half of the wall `AccessibilityBoundaryTests` ports, pinned by that suite's `theScannerRunsInCI`) on push/PR to `main` (Ubuntu, no Xcode). |

## Dependency

| Path | Purpose |
| --- | --- |
| `Fernlet/FernletKit/` | The local Swift package holding the carved-up on-device modules (see the FernletKit Package module map above). |
| CryptoSwift | Remote SPM dependency (`.upToNextMinor` from 1.10.0) supplying the Scrypt KDF used by `FernletLock`; no longer checked into the repo. |
| ProximityKit | Remote SPM dependency from [bowman-mw/proximitykit](https://github.com/bowman-mw/proximitykit), pinned at exactly `0.1.1` in `FernletKit/Package.swift` and the app project alike (ProximityKit plan step A1.3 at `0.1.0`; `0.1.1` since 2026-10-17): the peer-to-peer subsystem, which FernletProximityUI, FernletConnections and FernletSocial depend on, and which the app and `FernletTests` both link. Allowlisted in `NoTrackingBoundaryTests` and [No-Tracking-Wall.md](No-Tracking-Wall.md). |
